# Lisa — R2 Implementation Plan

**Release:** R2  
**Plan revision:** 6 (2026-11-23) — **proposed**  
**Status:** This revision answers the owner's approval review of revision 5 (`8534ea6`). Revision 4 is `b2f67db`, revision 3 `202c600`, revision 2 `a882c26`, and the R1 implementation baseline is `491c90a`. When revision 6 began, HEAD was `8534ea6` with a clean worktree and no commits after revision 5. The optional review document "R2 revision 5 approval review.md" isn't on this account's filesystem; the owner's brief is self-contained and was used. Intervening temporary work is preserved untouched (§1.1). The plan returns to the owner for approval before M0 starts. No R2 implementation has started, and the M0 suite has not run.  
**Governing documents:** `CONSTRICTION.md`, which overrides everything else, including this plan; `specs/R2.md` (2026-11-16); `specs/R1.md`, whose requirements apply unless R2 or an owner decision replaces them (R2-SCOPE-02, -03); the owner's decisions recorded in §15, §16 and §22.

Approving this plan grants no permission that `CONSTRICTION.md` requires. §15 and §16 record every permission and decision.

## Reading guide

- **Independent of earlier revisions.** Every rule is stated here; nothing is delegated to an earlier revision. Revision 6 replaces in place the rules the approval review of revision 5 rejected, and keeps revision 5's working designs, section structure and evidence classes. Rules revision 3 had left as "as revision 2" were restored in place by revision 4: merge rules (§8.3.1), the operation catalog (§8.2.3), client store contents and states (§8.6.1), the reconciliation order (§8.6.5), the nearby session layer, failures and relay (§8.7), offline intelligence (§8.9.1), the notification handoff (§8.10.4), the v2 routes and errors (§8.12.1), the v1 route table (§8.12.2), the CLI with its modes (§8.12.3), the apps and Siri (§8.13), preparation for R1 data on a Mac (§8.14.1), migration and reverse migration (§8.14.2, §8.14.8), limits (§8.15.1), transfer rules (§8.16), source compatibility (§10.3) and controlled substitutes (§10.4).
- **Four kinds of statement are kept apart** (§7.4): *Required* (a requirement ID, or an owner requirement with its date), *Mechanism* (the chosen design), *Decision* (settled by the owner) and *Experiment* (still to run).
- **Observations** name their source, date, platform and revision (§1).
- **Proposed checks** are written in the future tense and carry scenario IDs (S-nn, N-A, N-B) or owner-harness case IDs (`OV-…`), defined in §10.2, §10.8 and §10.9 and recorded in `docs/VERIFICATION-SCENARIOS.md`. No result is claimed for a check that hasn't run.
- **Executor and cause.** Every status names who runs the check, Claude or the owner, and its actual cause (§10.1). Claude never uses the owner's accounts; an owner-run check waits on the owner's checklist with its own cause.
- **Recovery facts.** Each design that can be interrupted or left uncertain ends with four statements: the evidence that survives, who can act on it, the action that is safe after an interruption, and what the owner sees while the outcome is unknown. Where evidence can't establish more, the design reports the uncertainty and claims no more.

## Where revision 6 answers the approval review

| # | Blocker | Revision 5 counterexample | Durable evidence now required | Who can act | What the owner sees after a crash or an uncertain outcome | Revised sections | Deciding scenario |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Writer fencing; devices whose keys Core doesn't know | `owner-attested-isolated` accepted a removed VPN route as a fence, though the old Core's scheduler, connectors and local CLI could still write. An iPad that enrolled or rotated its key after the backup couldn't pass a challenge to deliver its newer roster | A writer fence that stops **every** write path and every restart: the old volume's lock and durable marker (`fence --local`, binding the volume's final head and journal position and exporting its unsynced tail), or the owner's attestation of a stop with restart prevented under the documented procedure. Client isolation is recorded as information only. A device's trust records arrive as a bounded **trust bundle**, by an owner-issued one-time code or a file, held in quarantine and verified from the anchored key history | The owner stops or fences the old Core and issues the code; Lisa verifies fence records and bundles; the device uploads | "Previous Lisa Core on H2 is cut off from your devices but may still change data on its own (its schedule, connectors and local commands). This Lisa Core accepts no changes until H2 is stopped with restart prevented." For a bundle: "Trust records from iPad: 1 revocation verified and applied; 1 roster unverified — needs the K1→K2 record." | §8.5.1, §8.5.3, §8.5.4, §8.1.5, §8.4.4 | S-10 steps 2–4 and 9; N-A steps 7–10 |
| 2 | Release of uncertain deliveries | A signed `clean-shutdown` record in an off-host copy was taken as the end of the old writer's journal, though a later run on the same volume could follow it. A submission started before the backup cut, whose outcome was never journaled, could be released from an otherwise complete suffix | A completeness proof only through a writer fence that **binds the old writer's final run, journal sequence and chain hash** (`fenced-local` or `fenced-on-host`). Every claim open at the cut is matched by its `claimID` to its journal start; a start without a proven outcome stays uncertain | Lisa releases only on that proof or on a device's report; otherwise the owner releases one slot at a time and records what the recipient side shows | "Held — the previous Lisa Core may have sent this; no fence binds its last journal entry." After a release whose own result was lost: "Released by you; outcome unknown. Reference R-7Q4K: check your inbox for it, then record what you found." | §8.10.6, §8.5.1, §8.4.4 | S-11 (d)–(h); N-A step 12 |
| 3 | The legacy converter's input range | 50 same-key preference changes exceeded the 32-dependency limit. A middle administrative child let later children run before the decision. A migrated R1 record has neither request bytes nor a per-change outcome. A relay received `r1Fingerprint`, a hash over Core-only values | Each child depends only on its **immediate** same-entity predecessor. The unprocessed suffix stays `pending_behind_review` until a CLI-only `apply` or `refuse`, while a frozen caller gets a consumable 503. **Outcome-only** migrated parents attach bytes only when Core's recomputed fingerprint matches. Replicated `legacyParent` drops the fingerprint, and restricted siblings replicate only signed, redacted receipts | Core converts; the owner decides held children at the administrative terminal; relays verify | A frozen client: "This change is waiting for review on the administrative terminal." In review: "R1 answered 409 for this request; which of its 3 changes R1 applied is unknown." | §8.12.2, §8.2.1, §8.14.2, §8.14.4, §8.17 | S-03 |
| 4 | Migration and downgrade evidence | A capture's title was never compared. A five-minute wall-clock search over paged lists was taken as proof that a task create failed. Reconciliation didn't consult the rollback cut before sending. Preference confirm and delete were reconciled as if revision-checked. Reverse migration wrote `{}`, which typed R1 clients can't decode | Capture verdicts cover every effect-bearing input, including whether the capture created its news item. A lost task create is resolved only by R1's own ledger record, read from the same database; otherwise it is indeterminate. The rollback archive's final dispositions and projected entities are checked before the first send. Set, confirm and delete each have their own rule. Every R1 reply is decodable, content-free and fits its route | Core classifies; the post-downgrade tool proves outcomes; the owner maps, re-enters or authorizes a resend | "Entry 421 (create task 'Call the plumber'): R1 may have created it; R1's record of this request is gone. One task with exactly this content exists (created <time by R1's clock>). Use it, send again (may duplicate), or skip." | §8.14.3, §8.14.7, §8.14.8, §8.12.2 | S-02, S-13; N-B |
| 5 | Calendar repair for readers and partitions | A repair at cut 120 could rewind a cursor that a concurrent page had moved to 150, and readers saw half-merged chunks. An older B snapshot could restore an event that A's newer snapshot proved absent | Pages for a repairing partition are **deferred**. Staged rows, membership, view and search entries stay invisible until one constant-size publication that re-checks epoch, coverage generation and cursors. A partition's proof through its cursor decides which shared rows count in it | The replica, automatically; nothing for the owner | "Updating — not yet verified" on the affected days until publication; never a half-merged day | §8.4.2, §8.4.3, §8.6.3 | S-08, S-06 |

The six related corrections, the owner's settled decisions, the required integrations and the owner-run harness are traced in §21 and §22. The integrations are designed in §8.19, the harness in §10.8, and the acceptance matrices are in §10.9. The table above is also returned with the plan.

## Where revision 5 answered the approval review of revision 4

| # | Blocker | Revision 4 counterexample | Revision 5 rule | Deciding acceptance case | Residual limitation |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Writer fence (§8.5.1–§8.5.5) | `credentials-rotated` was accepted as a fence, but a previous Core with rotated provider credentials can still accept stale clients' edits, sign receipts, run its scheduler and restart | A **writer fence** per previous Core (fenced locally, fenced on its own host, or owner-attested stopped or isolated under a defined procedure), independent of **sender fences** per channel. While any writer fence is unknown, only evidence collection runs: no confirmation, credential refresh or ordinary write. A head is accepted only with an owner confirmation, for descendant and sibling moves alike. The current signing key's private half on a fresh host comes from the owner's key backup, or re-anchoring follows | S-10: H2 unreachable from H3 while a stale client still writes to H2; provider credentials rotated; H3 refuses confirmation and writes until H2's writer fence exists, then confirms, refreshes credentials, replays and reviews delivery separately; a late, already-confirmed iPad with an invalidated bearer; a device alone holding post-backup K2 evidence | An owner attestation is trusted as stated; Lisa can't verify a host it can't reach |
| 2 | Release of uncertain deliveries (§8.10.6) | An intact off-host journal prefix plus a fence marker appended by the replacement was read as proof that a slot was unsent | Only the old writer's own **terminal checkpoint** (written by its clean shutdown or by `lisa core fence --local` on its volume) closes its journal. Slots it might have sent stay held across regeneration, resume and restart. Delegated push slots stay held until the device reports its local schedule. A release authorizes one more attempt and warns that the provider may already have accepted the earlier one | S-11: (a) SMTP accepts after the off-host copy, the volume is lost, the slot stays held after regeneration and resume; (b) a genuine terminal checkpoint proves a slot unsent; (c) a delegated firing scheduled by an offline phone stays held despite a complete journal showing no APNs submission; a crash during release | When the old volume is lost, held slots stay held until the owner releases them or they expire |
| 3 | Legacy requests (§8.2.1, §8.4.1, §8.12.2, §8.14.4–§8.14.5) | Different signed bodies under one operation ID were treated as duplicates when they declared the same R1 fingerprint | **One authoritative conversion at Core.** Children are Core-signed and a pure function of the original request bytes, so one ID always has one digest, on every replica and in every epoch. The parent binds scope, authority, fingerprint, the complete child set, order and R1's actual outcome. Devices upload original bytes, never derived operations. A lookup grant authorizes lookups only; conversion needs its own authority; owner-only keys go to administrative review | S-03: a multi-preference request with a repeated key and an owner-only key, imported in parts and out of order with a v1 retry between parts, interrupted after the first durable step, restarted, and repeated in reverse order; a partially committed R1 prefix; a different body declaring the same fingerprint; each child's effect and receipt checked, including by a relay | A relay checks signatures, ID derivation, sibling sets and order, but not the original R1 bytes, which only Core holds |
| 4 | Queue classification and post-downgrade reconciliation (§8.14.3, §8.14.7–§8.14.8) | A recorded 409 from a second attempt was classed "rejected, no effect" after a first attempt had committed; a capture key alone counted as proof; reconciliation could resend a task create whose answer was lost | **Unresolved attempt history** (uncertainty, requeue, several attempts) outranks automatic conversion and "rejected, no effect"; captures are matched by content as well as key; input is preserved per child for owner review. Reconciliation records intent and a stable entry identity before sending, deduplicates overlapping exports, maps created R1 entities for dependent edits, and proves each route's outcome or shows it as indeterminate before any further attempt | S-02: an earlier committed correction followed by a recorded 409; a capture under a lost intermediate requeue ID; an existing capture key with a different body and an expired ledger; processes killed after a task create, retried within and beyond R1's retention; two archives holding one operation | A task create whose answer is lost needs the owner when a candidate exists; R1 gives no other proof |
| 5 | Calendar interval coverage (§8.4.2–§8.4.3, §8.6.3) | Occurrences were bucketed by start day, so an event spanning days 29–31 left the replica when day 29 rolled out, though it still blocks day 30 | Occurrences belong to every UTC-day bucket their interval overlaps (a two-number range). Rows carry versions; each partition keeps its own cursor; pages and snapshots change only the partition they cover, row by row, never backward. A partition repair is staged, resumable, and switches its coverage in one transaction | S-08: a window rolled past day 29 with the event still blocking day 30; one expired day repaired while a neighbor, a pending task and its search result stay live; a page for (90,120] relayed to a device with A at 150 and B at 100; the repair interrupted and resumed | A repairing partition shows as unverified until its coverage switches |

Revision 5's related corrections (private-CA pinning, v1 replay for real callers, backups and compaction, deletion and export, Apple notifications and networking, bounded work, deployment and milestones) are listed in §21. Where revision 6 replaced one of these rules, the revision 6 table above and §20 say so.

### 1.3 Historical build probe (2026-11-19, Fedora 45, Swift 6.3.2, before the owner's library installation)

`swift build --force-resolved-versions --target <target>` compiled 16 header-free targets and their dependencies cleanly in debug. LisaSQLite, LisaXML, LisaCurl and everything above them failed for missing headers, which the owner then installed. The probe has not been repeated, and the R1 suite has not run here; M0 does both.

### 1.4 What R1's evidence establishes

- **arm64 (O-11):** `IMPLEMENTATION-REPORT.md` §1–§2 records R1 on Debian 13, aarch64, Swift 6.4.2: 376 tests in 66 suites passed in debug and in release, after `swift package clean`. That environment no longer exists. It is R1 evidence only; R2 arm64 build and run are `NOT_RUN_ENVIRONMENT` (Claude; missing platform) until a run exists, and a genuine cross-build would get its own evidence entry.
- **Fedora:** R1 has no evidence on Fedora 45, Swift 6.3.2, SQLite 3.53.4, libxml2 2.15.4, libcurl 8.21.0 or OpenSSL 4.0.3. M0 produces it.
- **Apple:** no R1 Apple build has ever run; R1's Apple code was only syntax-checked. Whether the R1 apps build at all is unknown (relevant to X7, §10.6).

### 1.5 Documentation and dependency research (R2-DEV-04)

Read from public sources; nothing private was sent.

| Source (date read) | What it establishes |
|---|---|
| TN3213, republished 2026-07-14 (read 2026-11-19) | Multipeer Connectivity is deprecated in Xcode 27. Network framework TLS-PSK "doesn't work with QUIC, it doesn't support TLS 1.3, and it only works with the older Network framework API". TLS-PKI with a local `SecIdentity` and custom trust is recommended; Apple platforms can't create a digital identity, and swift-certificates is named. Peer-to-peer Wi-Fi is opt-in; framing, flow control (chunks of at least 64 KiB), keepalives and send timeouts are the app's job |
| Security reference (2026-11-19) | No documented `tls_ciphersuite_t` PSK value. From iOS 12/macOS 10.14: local identity, verify block, required peer authentication, `add_pre_shared_key`, TLS server name, TLS exporter. From iOS 13/macOS 10.15: PSK selection block, negotiated suite and version |
| Network framework (2026-11-19) | `NWConnection.currentPath`, `NWPath.remoteEndpoint`, `NWParameters.preferNoProxies` exist from iOS 12/macOS 10.14. No public NAT64-prefix API was found |
| TN3151, revised 2026-07-23 (2026-11-19) | URLSession for HTTP; a simplistic HTTP client on a TCP API is acceptable for a specific gap; connect by name rather than resolving first; test DNS64/NAT64; use Apple's trust evaluation |
| TN3179 (2026-11-19) | Local network privacy from iOS 14/macOS 15; `NSBonjourServices`; a background operation while permission is undetermined is denied silently; denial shows as −65570 or `localNetworkDenied`; test on macOS 15.1 or later |
| `UNNotificationRequest.identifier` (2026-11-19) | Adding a request with an existing identifier replaces it |
| DNS Service Discovery C API (`dns_sd`, Apple reference, read 2026-11-21) | `DNSServiceGetAddrInfo` takes an interface index, and its reply callback, `DNSServiceGetAddrInfoReply`, receives the interface index and a 32-bit TTL with each address; `DNSServiceQueryRecord` does the same for any record type |
| Apple open-source `configd` (`nat64-configuration.c`, read 2026-11-21) | Calls `nw_nat64_copy_prefixes_async` with an interface index to learn NAT64 prefixes. Whether the public SDK exposes this function is unverified, so the plan doesn't depend on it; E-1 records whether it does (§8.9.3) |
| RFC 7050 (read 2026-11-21) | RFC §3: a node must use all learned Pref64::/n in the order received, check the well-known address on octet boundaries, cache replies, rediscover on a new network and ten seconds before the TTL expires. RFC §3.1: DNSSEC validation only shows the prefix belongs to the NAT64 FQDN's domain; it cannot apply to the well-known prefix; an unvalidated discovery is not secure. RFC §5.1: discovery "does not support learning of the possible rules used by a DNS64 server for mapping specific IPv4 address ranges to separate Pref64::/n prefixes" |
| swift-certificates (GitHub API, 2026-11-20) | 1.21.1 released 2026-10-26, commit `84f03fafeca28c19f5fc4b528df3d22ce8896bd7`, Apache-2.0, `swift-tools-version:6.1`, no platform restriction; requires swift-crypto `3.12.3..<6.0.0` (`Crypto`, `_CryptoExtras`) and swift-asn1 `from: 1.1.0`. On Darwin, `Certificate.PrivateKey` wraps `SecureEnclave.P256.Signing.PrivateKey` or `SecKey` |
| swift-crypto 5.1.1 checkout (`.build/checkouts`, 2026-11-20) | `Crypto` includes HPKE with the `P256_SHA256_AES_GCM_256` suite |
| `Apps/project.yml` (2026-11-20; re-read 2026-11-22) | Deployment targets iOS 18.0 and macOS 15.0; bundle IDs `org.example.lisa.mac` and `org.example.lisa.companion`; generated with XcodeGen 2.46.0 on a Mac; the team ID lives in the git-ignored `Apps/Config/Signing.local.xcconfig` |

**Provider documentation (read 2026-11-22).** These rows come from delegated, read-only research of public pages: no account was used and nothing private was sent. Items a provider doesn't document are marked, and become owner-session experiments (§10.5). The OpenAI pages came through a summarizing fetch, so their details are re-verified at M7.

| Source | What it establishes |
|---|---|
| Microsoft personal accounts (learn.microsoft.com: graph/permissions-reference, message-delta, event-delta, user-list-calendarview, outlook-get-shared-events-calendars, entra reference-breaking-changes, v2-oauth2-auth-code-flow, refresh-tokens, graph/throttling-limits; support.microsoft.com: "POP, IMAP, and SMTP settings for Outlook.com", "Sending limits in Outlook.com") | Outlook.com IMAP, POP and SMTP require OAuth 2.0 ("Outlook.com requires the use of Modern Auth / OAuth2"; basic authentication ended on 2024-09-16), and IMAP is off by default. Graph v1.0 grants personal accounts delegated `Mail.Read` (with message delta), `Mail.Send`, `Calendars.Read` and `Calendars.Read.Shared`; v1.0 event delta covers only the primary calendar; reading through Graph a calendar that one personal account shares with another is **not documented**. App registrations must live in a directory (since June 2024). The authorization code flow accepts `consumers`. Refresh tokens last 90 days and replace themselves on each use. Graph allows 10,000 requests per 10 minutes and 4 concurrent requests per app and mailbox. Free accounts' sending limits are unpublished |
| Google (developers.google.com: gmail/imap/imap-smtp, gmail/api/auth/scopes, users.history/list, identity/protocols/oauth2 and its native-app and limited-input-device pages, calendar/api/auth, calendar/api/guides/sync, calendar/caldav/v2/guide; support.google.com: cloud/answer/15549945, cloud/answer/13464325, mail/answer/7126229, accounts/answer/185833) | Gmail refuses password sign-in from third-party apps, and IMAP is always on. IMAP and SMTP with OAuth need the restricted, full-access `https://mail.google.com/` scope; `gmail.readonly` is restricted and `gmail.send` sensitive; `history.list` answers 404 for an expired history ID. An OAuth app with user type **Personal** authorizes only the account that created it, "do[es] not require OAuth app verification, [is] not subject to the OAuth user cap or 7-day test user token expiration"; a Testing-status app's tokens expire after 7 days. The device flow can't grant Gmail or Calendar scopes; out-of-band is gone; desktop clients use loopback with PKCE. Refresh tokens die after six months unused, on a password change (Gmail scopes), on revocation, or beyond 100 per client and account. `calendar.readonly` covers every calendar the account can access, shared ones included; an expired sync token returns 410; CalDAV accepts only OAuth. A personal Gmail account sends at most 500 messages a day |
| Apple iCloud (support.apple.com 102525, 102654, 102198 and the iCloud and Calendar user guides) | iCloud Mail: IMAP `imap.mail.me.com:993` with TLS and SMTP `smtp.mail.me.com:587` with STARTTLS, using an app-specific password, which needs two-factor authentication; 1,000 messages and 1,000 recipients a day. App-specific passwords let an app reach "mail, contacts, and calendars", can't be narrowed, number at most 25, and are all revoked when the account password changes. Apple **documents no CalDAV endpoint**, and doesn't say whether calendars shared with an account appear to third-party apps. Private sharing gives a read-only invitee who has an Apple Account; a public calendar is open to "Anyone you send the URL to" |
| Proton (proton.me support pages "subscribe-to-external-calendar", "share-calendar-via-link", "share-calendar-with-proton-users", "why-you-need-bridge", "operating-systems-supported-bridge", "email-sending-limits"; the 2026 roadmap posts; the ProtonMail Bridge repository) | Proton Mail Bridge, on paid plans only, serves IMAP (1143) and SMTP (1025) only "to applications running on the same device", with its own password and a certificate clients must trust. Its Linux packages target the latest Ubuntu LTS and Fedora on 64-bit x86; it needs a password store and must keep running. "Proton Calendar doesn't support CalDAV … The only option right now is to subscribe to a calendar via a shared link"; "Anyone with the shared calendar link can view the calendar", and a full-view link carries the decryption key; sharing with Proton users works only inside Proton's apps; no calendar API, CalDAV or Bridge calendar is announced |
| OpenAI (developers.openai.com, through a summarizing fetch) | Chat Completions "remains supported"; Structured Outputs with `response_format` `json_schema` and `strict: true`, refusals in `message.refusal`, truncation as `finish_reason: "length"`; `max_tokens` deprecated in favor of `max_completion_tokens`; newer models reject sampling parameters; usage in `prompt_tokens` and `completion_tokens`; errors 401, 403, 429 (a rate limit honoring `Retry-After`, or `insufficient_quota`), 500, 503; `x-request-id` on every response and an optional client `X-Client-Request-Id`; no idempotency key |
| Anthropic (platform.claude.com) | The Messages API (`POST /v1/messages`) with a dated `anthropic-version` (`2026-10-21` current, which rejects `temperature`, `top_p` and `top_k`); `max_tokens` required and counting thinking tokens; content parsed by block type; structured outputs generally available as `output_config.format` with `json_schema` (no recursive schemas, numeric or length constraints); `stop_reason` includes `refusal` (HTTP 200) and `max_tokens`; usage `input_tokens`, two cache fields and `output_tokens`; errors include 402 `billing_error`, 429 (with `retry-after`, except a spend-limit 429), 504 `timeout_error` and 529 `overloaded_error`; a `request-id` header. Its OpenAI compatibility layer "is not considered a long-term or production-ready solution" |
| Ollama (docs.ollama.com) | Native `POST /api/chat` with `format` (a JSON schema), `options` (including `num_ctx`; the default context is 4,096 tokens), `keep_alive`, and `stream` defaulting to true (an error mid-stream keeps HTTP 200); token counts in `prompt_eval_count` and `eval_count`; 404 for a missing model; 503 when the queue is full. It binds `127.0.0.1:11434` by default and has no authentication. `/v1/chat/completions` supports a subset, including `response_format` with a JSON schema but without `strict` |
| Twilio (twilio.com/docs: call-resource, iam/test-credentials, usage/trials, usage/webhooks, usage/security) | Calls are created with `To`, `From` and `Url` or `Twiml`; statuses queued, ringing, in-progress, completed, busy, failed, no-answer and canceled; status callbacks need a reachable URL; the Calls resource documents no idempotency key; test credentials accept magic numbers, never place a real call and don't simulate call progress; trial accounts call only verified numbers |

### 1.6 R1 source facts that revisions 4–6 depend on (read at `202c600`, `b2f67db` and `8534ea6`, all equal to `491c90a` for `Sources/`, `Apps/`, `Tests/` and the package files — `git diff --stat 491c90a 8534ea6` lists no change there — on 2026-11-20, 2026-11-21 and 2026-11-22)

Paths are under `Sources/` unless they start with `Apps/` or `docs/`.

**API, ledger and errors**

| Fact | Evidence | Used in |
|---|---|---|
| Every `store.write` is its own `BEGIN IMMEDIATE … COMMIT` | `LisaSQLite/SQLiteDatabase.swift:63-76` | §8.12.2 |
| The ledger key is `<scope>:<requestID>` (`owner` or `device:<id>`); the fingerprint is SHA-256 over method, path, sorted query and body, not headers | `LisaAPI/APIRouter.swift:114-115, 174-181`; `LisaAPI/RouterContext.swift:80-87` | §8.12.2, §8.14.3 |
| Before the ledger: authentication, then throttling (429). A missing or invalid request ID is a 400 that is not recorded. A duplicate in flight is a 503 with retry 1 s. An oversized body is a recorded 413 | `APIRouter.swift:45-50, 109-113, 129-139` | §8.14.3 |
| The response is recorded in a later write (`RequestLedger.swift:35-52`) for 2xx, 400, 403, 404, 409 and 413 only. If that write fails, the client gets 503/500 although the mutation committed | `APIRouter.swift:144-148, 163-165`; `LisaApplication/RequestLedger.swift` | §8.12.2, §8.14.3 |
| After a route's main commit, propagation adds up to four commits (occurrence and terminal handling, `cancelAll`, `plans.compute`, `reminders.sync`) and a re-read. A failure there is a 503 or 500 that is not recorded, so a retry runs the handler again | `LisaApplication/LisaApplication.swift:112-150`; `LisaAPI/APIFailure.swift:50-54` | §8.12.2 |
| Task PATCH: revision check; one correction write (estimate and title validated first; notes, priority and confirmation unchecked) with a `task.corrected` audit, then propagation; then a separate status write and propagation. "the task cannot move from … to …" can only come from the status step; "the item changed since it was read; reload it and try again" and 404 cannot be attributed to a step | `LisaAPI/WorkHandlers.swift:61-85`; `LisaOwnerData/TaskService.swift:96-159`; `LisaDomain/LisaTask.swift:124-134` | §8.12.2, §8.14.3 |
| Idea PATCH has the same two-step shape; tags are part of the edit; the summary is trimmed and cut to 500 characters; planning can raise the idea's revision between the steps | `WorkHandlers.swift:96-118`; `LisaOwnerData/IdeaService.swift:48-108`; `LisaAgenda/PlanService.swift:153-167` | §8.12.2 |
| Preferences PATCH applies 1–50 changes in order, each a write plus propagation; the first error stops it, earlier changes stay, and the error does not say which change failed. Devices get 403 for owner-only keys and for any delete. The success body lists every preference, including addresses and endpoints | `LisaAPI/SettingsHandlers.swift:16-38`; `LisaOwnerData/PreferenceService.swift:34-114`; `LisaOwnerData/DeletionService.swift:45-47` | §8.12.2, §8.14.4 |
| Notification action: a paired device's receipt is written first; then the reminder action (and, for "complete", the task transition and propagation) or, for other subjects, resolve plus audit | `LisaApplication/ApplicationCommands.swift:93-126` | §8.12.2 |
| `createTask` failures after its commit are not recorded, so a retry creates a second task. Capture is one write and also deduplicates permanently by caller and request ID | `ApplicationCommands.swift:30-34`; `LisaOwnerData/CaptureService.swift:67-102` | §8.12.2, §8.14.3 |
| Error codes are exactly `invalidRequest` 400, `unauthenticated` 401, `forbidden` 403, `notFound` 404, `conflict` 409, `payloadTooLarge` 413, `requestIDReused` 422, `throttled` 429, `unavailable` 503, `internalError` 500 | `LisaAPIContract/APIError.swift:6-44` | §8.12.2 |
| Every response carries `Lisa-API-Version: 1.0`; replays add `Lisa-Replayed: true`. There is no 426 and no version negotiation | `LisaAPI/RouterSupport.swift:52-54`; `APIRouter.swift:118-120` | §8.12.2 |
| Ledger records hold scoped key, fingerprint, status, response body and expiry; expiry is 30–3650 days (default 30); pruning runs in daily maintenance after retention. Deleting an entity never purges or redacts a stored response | `LisaPorts/IdempotencyRecord.swift:10-42`; `RequestLedger.swift:20-65`; `LisaRuntime/HostRuntime.swift:131-137`; `LisaConfig/APIConfiguration.swift:46-82` | §8.12.2, §8.17 |
| *(read for revision 5, 2026-11-21)* A task PATCH whose first attempt committed its correction but whose answer was never recorded (crash, or a failed ledger write) meets the revision check on a retry with the same request ID, and that 409 is recorded under the same key. A recorded 409 therefore proves only what the attempt that produced it did | `WorkHandlers.swift:61-85`; `APIRouter.swift:108-150` | §8.14.3 |
| *(revision 5)* A capture retry is answered from the capture's permanent caller-scoped key, whatever the body, once the ledger record is gone: 200 with the existing capture and `created: false`. A second retrieval of a page already read is a 409 | `LisaOwnerData/CaptureService.swift:67-78`; `LisaApplication/CapturedPageService.swift:16, 116` | §8.12.2, §8.14.3 |
| *(revision 5)* R1 creates ideas only through captures of kind `idea`, in the capture's transaction; there is no idea-create route | `CaptureService.swift:84-89`; `LisaAPIContract/APIRoute.swift` | §8.14.8 |
| *(revision 5)* Notification receipts: at most 200 IDs, each recorded in its own write in request order; unknown IDs are ignored; only a storage failure stops the loop, leaving the earlier receipts committed and answering an unrecorded 503/500. The client removes repeated IDs before queueing | `LisaAPI/DeviceHandlers.swift:88-99`; `LisaApplication/ApplicationDevices.swift:52-59`; `ApplicationCommands.swift:121-126`; `LisaDelivery/DeliveryOutbox.swift:163-177`; `LisaClient/NotificationActionHandler.swift:85-112` | §8.12.2 |
| *(revision 5)* API request bodies are at most 262,144 bytes, but task and idea notes are stored without a length check, so paths that bypass the API (extraction, import) can store longer notes | `LisaAPIContract/APIVersioning.swift:17`; `TaskService.swift:103-149` | §8.3.3, §8.14.2 |
| *(read for revision 6, 2026-11-22; `git diff --stat 491c90a 8534ea6` lists no change under `Sources/`, `Apps/`, `Tests/` or the package files)* Preferences: a `set` with `expectedRevision` checks it only when the key exists, so it inserts an absent key; `confirm` and `delete` take no revision; `confirm` on an absent key is a 404 | `LisaAPI/SettingsHandlers.swift:18-38`; `LisaOwnerData/PreferenceService.swift:27-116` | §8.14.8 |
| *(revision 6)* Recorded errors can echo caller input: "unknown notification category <name>", "unknown weekday <token>", an unsupported preference key as both `field` and message, and an owner-only key named in the 403. 400 and 403 are recorded statuses | `LisaAPI/DeviceHandlers.swift:76`; `LisaOwnerData/PreferenceMapping.swift:97-98, 175-189`; `LisaAPI/APIFailure.swift:58-63`; `LisaOwnerData/PreferenceService.swift:34-35`; `APIRouter.swift:163-165` | §8.12.2 |
| *(revision 6)* The ledger lookup ignores a record past its expiry, and maintenance deletes it. R1's not-found answer reads "the task does not exist" (or the idea's, reminder's …) | `LisaApplication/RequestLedger.swift:18-65`; `LisaRuntime/HostRuntime.swift:131-134`; `LisaAPI/APIFailure.swift:18-20, 42-43` | §8.12.2, §8.14.8 |

**R1 clients**

| Fact | Evidence | Used in |
|---|---|---|
| A response whose `Lisa-API-Version` major is not 1 becomes `incompatibleVersion`, checked before the status; a missing header is accepted. In the queue that counts as "may have been applied" | `LisaClient/ResponseInterpreter.swift:9-22`; `LisaClient/LisaAPIClient.swift:95-108`; `LisaClient/QueueSendOutcome.swift:41-42` | §8.12.2 |
| An error body must decode with one of the ten codes and a message, or the whole body is dropped and the error is `unexpectedResponse(status)`. A 426 is therefore shown as "That didn't work: the Lisa host answered with the unexpected status 426." unless its code is one of the ten | `LisaAPIContract/APIError.swift:6-51`; `LisaClient/ClientError.swift:67`; `LisaPresentation/ActionOutcome.swift:25-39` | §8.12.2 |
| Queue outcomes: 2xx done (body stored raw, decoded only with `try?` for the just-submitted entry); 409 conflict; 401 interrupted and not applied; 429/502/503/504 retry later; every other status failed; interrupted transport, cancellation and version mismatch are "may have been applied"; unreachable, refused, not paired and credential errors are "not applied" | `QueueSendOutcome.swift:22-59`; `LisaPresentation/ActionSubmitter.swift:39-42, 104-108` | §8.14.3 |
| `attempts` is incremented and saved before each send, including sends that never leave the device. `lastError` keeps only the latest answer. `uncertainSince` is set once and cleared only by requeue; a 10 s timeout counts as uncertain. Loading turns `sending` into `pending` with `uncertainSince` | `LisaClient/OfflineQueue+Flush.swift:75-134`; `LisaClient/OfflineQueue.swift:36-42` | §8.14.3 |
| Requeue (allowed for conflict and failed) assigns a new random request ID and resets attempts, errors and uncertainty; earlier request IDs are not recorded anywhere. `requestID` differs from `id` only after a requeue | `OfflineQueue.swift:154-183`; `LisaClient/QueuedRequest.swift:107-117` | §8.14.3 |
| An uncertain entry fails after `maximumRetryPeriod` (30 days, equal to the ledger retention). Done entries are pruned after an hour | `OfflineQueue+Flush.swift:75-82, 172-174`; `LisaClient/OfflineQueueConfiguration.swift:25-40` | §8.12.2, §8.14.3 |
| The queue file is `<Client>/queue/queue.json`, format `lisa-client-queue` version 1, fields id, requestID, kind, method, path, body, createdAt, status, attempts, lastAttemptAt, nextAttemptAt, uncertainSince, lastError {status, code, message, field}, serverRevision, response {status, body}, completedAt; owner-only, replaced atomically. Any other format or version stops the R1 app from starting | `LisaClient/FileQueueStorage.swift:8-48`; `LisaPresentation/ClientServices.swift:51-55`; `LisaClient/StorageCoding.swift:27-42` | §8.14.3–§8.14.5 |
| The R1 Mac app is not sandboxed (D-50). It embeds the host in process as the trusted local owner, also serving the device listener and a loopback owner listener (`run/owner-endpoint.json`); if `lisa serve` holds the lock it is a loopback owner client instead. Its queue is `~/Library/Application Support/org.example.lisa.mac/Client/queue/queue.json`. It has no pending-actions screen | `Apps/Config/Mac-Release.entitlements`; `docs/DECISIONS.md` D-50; `Apps/Mac/Host/EmbeddedHostController.swift:58-92`; `Apps/Mac/Host/ServeClientConnection.swift:32-57`; `Apps/Shared/Storage/ProtectedStorageLocation.swift:34-47`; `Apps/Mac/Settings/DiagnosticsView.swift:141-167` | §8.14.4 |
| The R1 companion speaks only `/v1/` (hard-coded paths, transport refuses others, major version 1). It pairs with a code, keeps `{installationID, deviceID, credential}` in a Keychain item (after first unlock, this device only), registers push directly (never queued), and trusts up to eight owner-supplied DER anchors plus an optional certificate pin | `LisaAPIContract/APIRoute.swift:55-88`; `LisaClient/HostEndpoint.swift:6-14, 85-89`; `Apps/Shared/Keychain/KeychainCredentialStore.swift:8-68`; `LisaPresentation/PushRegistrationModel.swift:9-92`; `LisaPresentation/HostTrustSettings.swift:6-35` | §8.14.5 |
| R1's Apple trust evaluator uses only owner-supplied anchors, checks the host name, fetches nothing, and can pin the leaf certificate's SHA-256 | `Apps/Shared/Trust/PinnedServerTrustEvaluator.swift` | §8.1.2 |
| *(revision 5)* The queue holds nine kinds: capture, task update, reminder action, notification action, proposal action, plan-block action, preference change, notification-preference change and notification receipts. Task creation and idea edits are never queued | `LisaClient/QueuedMutation.swift:6-17` | §8.12.2 |
| *(revision 5)* Typed client calls decode the success body strictly and report "the Lisa host's response could not be read" on failure. `lisa task add` calls `createTask` with a new random request ID and decodes a `TaskDTO`; `lisa capture --request-id` decodes a `CaptureDTO`, and `--retrieve` derives `<id>:retrieve`. When the queue refuses an action, `DirectMutationSender` sends it once with a new random ID | `LisaClient/LisaAPIClient.swift:65-72`; `LisaClient/ResponseInterpreter.swift:33-41`; `LisaCLI/TaskCommand.swift:71-82`; `LisaCLI/CaptureCommand.swift:51-94`; `LisaPresentation/DirectMutationSender.swift:7-33` | §8.12.2 |
| *(revision 5)* `CaptureDTO` carries no text: ID, kind, capture time, processing state, idea and news IDs, and `created` | `LisaAPIContract/WorkDTOs.swift:243-266` | §8.12.2 |
| *(revision 6)* `TaskDTO` carries the title and notes, so a content-free `TaskDTO` can't exist | `LisaAPIContract/WorkDTOs.swift:30-50` | §8.12.2 |
| *(revision 6)* A capture's title takes effect only sometimes: for kinds text, link and Apple News, R1 names the news item with the supplied title only when this capture creates the item; a capture of a URL already saved links to the existing item, whose title stays. The item's evidence records the creating capture's ID, and shared-content items are never pruned. An idea ignores the title | `LisaOwnerData/CaptureService.swift:84-94, 134-157`; `LisaOwnerData/RetentionService.swift:78-88` | §8.14.3 |
| *(revision 6)* R1's adapters: IMAP logs in with a password, opens folders with `EXAMINE` and fetches with `BODY.PEEK[]` only; SMTP uses a password; a calendar is a public HTTPS address or a secret URL; the model adapter sends `json_object`, a temperature and `stream: false`; a Twilio 2xx counts as accepted with its call SID, and no call status is read | `LisaIMAP/IMAPMailboxSettings.swift:18-19`; `LisaIMAP/IMAPCommand.swift:1-30`; `LisaSMTP/SMTPSettings.swift:21-22`; `LisaCalendarFeeds/CalendarSubscriptionAddress.swift:15-19`; `LisaOpenAICompatible/ChatCompletionsRequestBody.swift:1-59`; `LisaTwilio/TwilioResultClassification.swift:14-18` | §8.19 |

**Reminders and delivery**

| Fact | Evidence | Used in |
|---|---|---|
| Reminder keys: `deadline:task:<id>:<changeSequence>:<offset>`, `…:soon`, `dateonly:task:<id>:<seq>`, `overdue:task:<id>:<date>`, `block:<plan>:<block>:<startMs>`, `proposal:<id>:<date>`. Priority, deadline and confirmation changes advance the change sequence; a title change does not | `LisaPolicy/ReminderPlanner.swift:29-104`; `LisaOwnerData/TaskService.swift:102-131` | §8.10.1 |
| Each firing is a notification intent keyed `reminder:<reminderID>:<firingMs>`; a snooze fires again at the snooze instant under a new key. Commitment changes are `commitmentChange:<subject>:<seq>`; briefs `brief:<date>:<zone>`. Deliveries are keyed `notify:<key>:device:<id>`, `email:<key>:<address>`, `brief-email:…`, `call:task:<id>:<key>` | `LisaAgenda/NotificationService.swift:24-31, 51-69`; `LisaDomain/Reminder.swift:117-120`; `LisaAgenda/ReminderService.swift:192, 217-238`; `LisaDomain/NotificationIntent.swift:104-112`; `LisaDelivery/DeliveryOutbox.swift:108-110`; `LisaDelivery/DeliveryProcessing.swift:169-171` | §8.10.1 |
| Delivery states: pending, suppressed, inProgress (phases preparing and submitting), acceptedByProvider, confirmedDelivered, failed, outcomeUnknown, cancelled (expiry is `cancelled` with reason "expired"), capturedDryRun. A restart turns submitting into outcomeUnknown and preparing back into pending. Leases default to 120 s; five attempts with backoff; no retry past `validUntil` | `LisaDomain/Delivery.swift:29-53`; `DeliveryOutbox.swift:197-262`; `DeliveryProcessing.swift:205-343` | §8.10, §8.5 |
| Reminder due checks, leases and validity use the wall clock; nothing handles a backward step | `LisaTime/WallClock.swift:6-17`; `ReminderService.swift:165-189` | §8.10, §8.11.4 |
| R1 has no client-side scheduling: the Mac's embedded host posts each local notification when due (no trigger). The 32-request limit comes from R2-NOT-02 | `Apps/Mac/Notifications/UserNotificationTransport.swift:8-43` | §8.10.5 |
| *(revision 5)* Calendar expansion keeps every occurrence that overlaps the window: timed events as `[start, end)`, with a zero-length event kept when its start is inside the window; all-day events from the start of their first day to the start of the day after, in the floating zone | `LisaICalendar/CalendarExpander.swift:288-298` | §8.4.2 |

**Retention, deletion, ingestion and operations**

| Fact | Evidence | Used in |
|---|---|---|
| Retention prunes raw content (90 days and a 500 MB cap), final deliveries (180 days by next-attempt time), audit events including `data.deleted` (365 days) and unreferenced feed items (30 days). Captures, ideas, tasks, proposals, emails, reminders and intents are never pruned by age | `LisaOwnerData/RetentionService.swift:55-106` | §8.14.3, §8.17 |
| Deletion is a hard delete with no tombstone. It is reachable in production only for preferences (`lisa preference delete` and the PATCH); capture, idea and task deletion exist only for tests. Reminder titles and intent title hints survive a delete | `LisaOwnerData/DeletionService.swift:11-182`; `LisaCLI/PreferenceCommand.swift:81-95` | §8.14.3, §8.17 |
| Mail ingestion inserts event proposals directly (`MailBatchProcessor.commit`) inside the connector's write transaction, which also advances the source cursor, with no principal and no audit. Extraction creates and updates tasks and proposals as the model; planning updates idea fields; rematching updates proposal match state | `LisaIngestion/MailIngestion.swift:113-169, 217-243`; `LisaIngestion/ConnectorRunner.swift:94-108`; `LisaReview/ExtractionService.swift:82-188`; `LisaAgenda/PlanService.swift:153-168`; `LisaReview/ProposalService.swift:27-48` | §8.3.5 |
| `ownerCorrected` on tasks covers title, priority, duration, confirmation, deadline and (for extracted tasks) status. No entity flags notes; the model writes task notes only at creation | `LisaDomain/LisaTask.swift:58-91`; `TaskService.swift:103-149` | §8.14.2 |
| `lisa backup` needs the exclusive lock, opens the host (running migrations and recovery), uses SQLite's online backup, and writes a manifest with format, schema, time, version and counts but no checksums. `lisa restore` checks the manifest, schema, integrity and `user_version`, moves the old files aside and copies the backup in non-atomically, leaving `runtime-state.json` and `model-requests.json` untouched | `LisaCLI/BackupCommand.swift:15-22`; `LisaStore/SQLiteStoreMaintenance.swift:27-48`; `LisaStore/StoreRestore.swift:17-59` | §8.4.4, §8.14 |
| Data directory: `lisa.sqlite` (WAL), `lisa.lock` (`flock`, recording pid, time and mode), `runtime-state.json`, `model-requests.json`, `dry-run/captures.jsonl`, `logs/`, `run/owner-endpoint.json`, pre-migration copies | `LisaHost/LisaHost.swift:44-70`; `LisaRuntime/DataDirectoryLock.swift:52-77` | §8.11.5, §8.14.1 |
| `serve`, `run-once`, `import`, `backup` and `restore` take the exclusive lock. Owner commands use the running host's loopback owner listener if one holds the lock, otherwise they open the host in process; `lisa status` therefore writes (recovery) when no host runs. Health lives only in memory | `LisaCLI/HostSession.swift:105-150`; `LisaAPI/SettingsHandlers.swift:46-86`; `LisaRuntime/HostRuntime.swift:65-148` | §8.11.4, §8.11.5 |
| File secrets must be regular files owned by the effective user with no group or other bits, at most 64 KiB; configuration holds only `env:`, `file:` or `keychain:` references | `LisaSecrets/ProtectedFileSecretStore.swift:16-32`; `LisaConfig/SecretReference.swift:12-29` | §8.11.2 |
| The bind key is `api.bindAddress` (one IP literal, default 127.0.0.1, port 8765); TLS is `none` (loopback only), `native` or `reverseProxy` (loopback or private only); configuration schema 1 | `LisaConfig/APIConfiguration.swift:6-57`; `LisaConfig/ConfigurationValidator.swift:23-53` | §8.11.3 |

**Network policy**

| Fact | Evidence | Used in |
|---|---|---|
| libcurl's address gate checks every resolved address before opening a socket (skipping refused ones) and the connected address before sending; proxies are disabled; TLS 1.2 or later with peer and host verification; a non-idempotent request is sent at most once per exchange | `LisaCurl/CurlAddressGate.swift:1-56`; `LisaCurl/CurlHTTPClient.swift:91, 135-139` | §8.9.3 |
| Model calls follow no redirects; feeds and pages follow at most 3 by default (at most 10), re-checking each hop and dropping credentials when leaving the origin | `LisaOpenAICompatible/OpenAICompatibleProvider.swift:74`; `LisaNewsFeeds/NewsFeedOptions.swift:24, 40`; `LisaHTTP/HTTPRedirects.swift:1-27` | §8.9.3 |
| Model endpoints: plain `http` is loopback-only; `localhost` and loopback or private IP literals may use local networks; every other host is public-only | `LisaHost/ConfiguredHostScope.swift:9-23` | §8.9.3 |
| The address policy decodes `::ffff:0:0/96` and `64:ff9b::/96` only | `LisaHTTP/NetworkAddress.swift:42-93` | §8.9.3 |

### 1.7 R1 inventory (R2-GOV-01 step 4)

| Area | What R1 has | R1 verification | What R2 does with it |
|---|---|---|---|
| Domain, time and engines (LisaDomain, LisaTime, LisaICalendar, LisaMIME, LisaXML, LisaFeeds, LisaHTMLText, LisaPlanning, LisaPolicy, LisaMatching, LisaRanking, LisaBriefComposer, LisaInference) | 17 entity types with one revision counter per record; pure planner, composer and policy | Debian/aarch64 | Shared by Core and clients; extended (§7.2.2) |
| Persistence (LisaSQLite, LisaStore, LisaPorts) | 18 tables, schema v4, one serialized connection, WAL with `synchronous=FULL`, hard deletes, online backup without checksums | Debian/aarch64 | Schema v5 (§8.4) |
| Services (LisaOwnerData, LisaReview, LisaAgenda, LisaDelivery, LisaDevices, LisaIngestion, LisaApplication) | Direct record writes with follow-up commits; outbox with leases and unknown outcomes; in-memory pairing codes; bearer credentials only | Debian/aarch64 | Operations and the authority path (§6, §8.3.5) |
| API (LisaAPIContract, LisaAPI, LisaAPIServer) | v1 only; ledger written after the mutation; NIO HTTP/1.1 with native TLS | Debian/aarch64, loopback | v1 translation (§8.12.2) and v2 (§8.12.1) |
| Host, runtime, configuration, CLI | Composition root, `flock`, 5-minute cycles, in-memory health, JSON5 configuration v1 | Debian/aarch64 | Lisa Core (§8.11) |
| Adapters (LisaCurl, LisaIMAP, LisaSMTP, LisaCalendarFeeds, LisaNewsFeeds, LisaXSource, LisaDiscordSource, LisaTwilio, LisaAPNs, LisaOpenAICompatible) | Production adapters, connect-time address safety | Scripted and loopback only | Core-only; LisaOpenAICompatible also on clients (§8.9.2) |
| Client (LisaClient) | v1 typed client, JSON-file offline queue, file cache | Integration tests | Kept for v1, the CLI, queue import and the shared Core transport (§8.6.5) |
| Presentation (LisaPresentation) | 15 view models driven by host calls | Temporary checks only | Rebuilt on the client core |
| Apple apps (`Apps/`) | Mac app embeds the host; companion; six App Intents; no background tasks, Bonjour, app icon or privacy manifest | Syntax-checked only | Major refactor (§8.13) |
| Persistent suite (`Tests/LisaTests/`) | 53 files drive the in-process host; 17 register the Mac's local-notification installation; one requires `GET /v2/tasks` to be a typed 404 | Passed on Debian/aarch64 | Run unchanged (§10.3) |

**R1 gaps that R2 requirements cover:** spoken brief uncertainty (R2-WORK-02, M5); owner-held blocks left after completion (R2-L27, M8); late-eligible devices (R2-NOT-01, M8); token feedback applied to the wrong token (M8); deletion unreachable through the API (R2-WORK-04, M3); the non-atomic ledger (R2-SYNC-03, M3); the model budget zone (R2-L35, M7); in-memory pairing codes (R2-TRUST-01, M3); device-side removal of resolved notifications (R2-NOT-05, M8); backups without checksums (R2-CORE-06, M3); health that writes or lives only in memory (R2-CORE-05, M3); documentation that disagrees with code (M9).

## 2. Objective

R2 turns Lisa into a distributed system with one authoritative service:
- **Lisa Core** runs in Docker on the owner's Linux server behind the owner's VPN. It owns the canonical dataset, the connectors, the scheduler and every email, call and push.
- **Lisa for Mac, iPhone and iPad** are clients. Each keeps a durable local database, works fully offline (R2-CAP-03), applies the owner's changes at once, synchronizes with Core over HTTPS and with nearby enrolled devices without Internet, and uses an explicitly configured model provider when Core is unreachable.
- **Core's connectors and senders** cover the owner's required providers — personal Outlook.com, Gmail with Google Calendar, iCloud Mail and Calendar, Proton Mail and Calendar, OpenAI, Anthropic, a self-hosted Ollama and Twilio — through dedicated Lisa accounts and documented, authenticated routes (§8.19). An owner-run harness verifies them and the Apple apps under the owner's own identity (§10.8).

R2 succeeds as R2 §20 defines: in every connectivity condition, and after a Core restart or restore, owner work is preserved, uncertainty stays visible, state reconciles without silent loss, and the boundaries around personal data and external actions hold.

## 3. Working constraints

`CONSTRICTION.md` overrides R2, this plan and every other instruction:
- **Git:** stay on `feature/distributive`; use only `git status`, `diff`, `log`, `show`, `rev-parse`, `add` and `commit`; never branch, push, pull, fetch, merge, rebase, cherry-pick, stash, clean, archive, amend or run `git config`; consult only this branch's history.
- **Commits:** one file-level operation per commit, staging only that file; implementation and documentation in separate commits; `add:` / `change:` / `remove:` messages. §13 says how multi-file changes stay buildable.
- **Modularity:** one responsibility per file and module; a protocol at every platform or service boundary (§7.2.3); no catch-all utility file; an acyclic graph; Swift 6 access control and concurrency safety.
- **Platforms:** the portable package imports no Apple-only framework; Apple code is only syntax-checked here until an owner session reports a build or run; no mock, stub or Linux-only substitute replaces required production behavior.
- **Tests (O-5, O-6 and its extension):** the R1 suite runs unchanged; R2 implementation harnesses are temporary, in a fresh `.tmp/r2/<run-id>/` per run; frozen R1 artifacts and scenario records as §10.2 describes. The one persistent addition is the owner-verification harness the owner explicitly requested, authored independently of the temporary harnesses (§10.8).
- **Temporary files:** only under `.tmp/` at the project root; never removed, overwritten, renamed or repurposed.
- **Environment:** the installed Swift 6.3.2 with SwiftPM; containers only through the owner's rootless engine within P-02/P-03; no `sudo`; no new dependency, image publication, production deployment or live external action without its own permission; `Package.resolved` preserved.
- **Security and privacy:** no secrets in source, fixtures, documentation or history; synthetic data only; no live accounts — Claude uses none of the owner's accounts, credentials, sessions or connected-account tools (owner decision, 2026-11-22); nothing private sent outside.
- **Reporting:** R2's statuses (`PASS`, `FAIL`, `NOT_RUN_ENVIRONMENT`, `NOT_RUN_CREDENTIALS`, `BLOCKED_PERMISSION`, `OUT_OF_SCOPE`) with the executor and the actual cause, the result levels of integrations kept apart, and the evidence classes of §10.1 kept apart.

## 4. Conflicts and how they are resolved

| # | R2 asks for | Constraint or evidence | Resolution |
|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | Portable builds and R1 regression | Headers were missing (§1.3) | Closed (historical): installed by the owner. M0 runs the unmodified suite first |
| C2 | Container deliverables and tests (R2-CORE-01, R2-D01–D12, R2-L33) | Only the rootless engine is usable; arm64 is unavailable; the engine has no SELinux labelling | P-02/P-03 authorize the rootless engine. First real use at M2 (tmpfs exhaustion runs); first production image at M4. arm64 `NOT_RUN_ENVIRONMENT` (Claude; missing platform). SELinux confinement and VPN reachability are target-host evidence (§10.1) |
| C3 | Mandatory acceptance cases (R2 §18) | Persistent suites only on explicit request | Temporary per-run harnesses, frozen R1 artifacts and tracked scenario records (O-6). The owner-verification harness is the persistent harness the owner explicitly requested (O-6 extension, §10.8), authored without reference to the temporary ones |
| C4 | R1 regression where R2 replaces behavior | Tests may not be changed to make code pass | Unchanged suite; classified failures; P-07 for any change request |
| C5 | Apple builds and device evidence | No Apple SDK here | Owner sessions X1–X7 on the owner's hardware, each with a buildable entry point (§10.6) |
| C6 | Live checks and deployment | Claude may use none of the owner's accounts (owner decision, 2026-11-22); production deployment and live actions keep their own scopes | The owner runs every credentialed and real-service check with the owner harness (§10.8); Claude verifies production code against fixtures and controlled servers; each status names its executor and cause (§10.1) |
| C7 | A resumable record (R2-DEV-03) | Temporary files can't be overwritten | Tracked `R2-CHECKPOINT.md` from M0 (O-7) |
| C8 | A pinned toolchain | Host evidence uses Swift 6.3.2 | Code builds with 6.3.2 and 6.4.2; images use 6.4.2 by digest (O-3) |
| C9 | A sandboxed Mac (O-9) that imports the old queue | The old queue lives outside the container | Owner-selected, read-only import through an open panel (§8.14.4) |
| C10 | Production device certificates if N-1 selects mutual TLS | swift-certificates is not approved | X1 uses proof-only provisioning; production issuance waits for P-08 (§8.7) |
| C11 | Frozen R1 artifacts from a fresh checkout | `git archive` and checkouts of other commits are not permitted | R1 sources are reconstructed with `git show 491c90a:<path>` into a run directory (§8.18) |
| C12 | An old-Mac upgrade rehearsal (O-9) | R1's Apple apps were never built | X7 needs an R1 Mac build made by the owner from the reconstructed tree, or uses the fixture path and reports the genuine-app part as `NOT_RUN_ENVIRONMENT` (§10.6) |
| C13 | R1's destination policy on Apple (R2-INT-03) | TN3151 advises connecting by name; R1's gate checks every address before a socket opens | The Apple path resolves first and connects only to checked addresses (§8.9.3); E-1 decides feasibility |
| C14 | Client public retrieval is required in the "Internet, Core unavailable" mode (R2-CAP-02); E-1 is a release requirement (owner decision, 2026-11-22) | E-1 may show it can't be done safely on a platform | Deferral to Core is a runtime fallback only; that platform's gates stay open, and a compliant redesign within the same policy is planned and proved there (§8.9.3, §22) |
| C15 | Authenticated, private, read-only access to four providers' calendars (owner requirement, 2026-11-22) | Proton Calendar has no API, CalDAV or Bridge calendar, and its links are readable by anyone holding them (§1.5) | The Proton Calendar row stays open with the evidence; changing the requirement is the owner's decision (§22) |
| C16 | Proton Mail ingestion and sending | Proton Mail Bridge must run beside its client and isn't an approved component | The Bridge sidecar is brought for approval as P-09; until then the Proton Mail rows are `BLOCKED_PERMISSION` for implementation (Claude; P-09 not granted) (§15, §22) |
| C17 | Provider access, while R2-SCOPE-02 forbids access to the owner's personal mailbox and keeps calendar subscriptions read-only, and `CONSTRICTION.md` forbids silently turning subscribed sources into account access | Provider credentials can't be narrowed: an iCloud app-specific password and every IMAP scope reach a whole account | Each provider connects a **dedicated Lisa account**; the owner shares calendars to it read-only; no connector signs in to the owner's personal account (§8.19.1) |

## 5. R1 → R2 replacement matrix (R2-SCOPE-03)

| R1 behavior | R2 implementation | R1 requirements affected |
|---|---|---|
| A Mac is the authoritative host | Lisa Core (Linux, Docker); `LisaHost` becomes its composition root | ARCH-03 replaced; one writer kept (R2-CORE-04) |
| The Mac app embeds the host | The Mac app is a sandboxed client linking no Core module | UI-01 kept; ARCH-03 replaced |
| Companion storage is a bounded cache | A durable SQLite replica per client with a journal and a bounded working set | UI-03 extended |
| Clients queue changes offline | Changes apply locally at once and reconcile later | UI-03, API-03 extended |
| Sync only between client and host | Core sync plus authenticated nearby sync and relay | NOTIFY-07 extended |
| No cloud or server sync | Sync with the owner's private Core over the owner's VPN | R1 §3.1 exclusion replaced |
| Only the host schedules notifications; companions schedule none (R1 plan §6.6) | Core decides; devices carry out delegated and locally created schedules under ownership records | NOTIFY-01, NOTIFY-03 extended; R1 plan §6.6 replaced |
| No client-side model | Direct client inference, explicitly enabled | MODEL-01, MODEL-04 extended |
| One OpenAI-compatible model adapter | OpenAI (Chat Completions with Structured Outputs), Anthropic (native Messages API) and a self-hosted Ollama (native chat API), on Core and as the authorized Apple fallback (§8.19.3) | MODEL-01, MODEL-02 extended (owner decision, 2026-11-22) |
| A dedicated mailbox reached by IMAP password login; outbound SMTP with a password | Provider routes for the dedicated mailbox: Microsoft Graph (Outlook.com), the Gmail API (Gmail), IMAP and SMTP with an app-specific password (iCloud), IMAP and SMTP through Proton Mail Bridge (Proton); read-only at the provider wherever a read-only scope exists (§8.19.2) | MAIL-01, MAIL-02 and EMAIL-01 kept ("provider-specific authentication", "a documented equivalent mail-provider adapter"); the transport replaced per provider |
| Calendars read from public or secret HTTPS iCalendar URLs | Authenticated, private, read-only provider access to calendars shared with the dedicated account (Graph, the Calendar API, CalDAV); links anyone can open refused for the four providers (§8.19.2) | CAL-01's secret-URL transport replaced for the required providers (owner decision, 2026-11-22); its recurrence, zone and conservative-planning rules kept |
| The host depends on the Mac staying awake | Core runs on its own | NOTIFY-07 statements replaced |
| A persistent test suite | Temporary harnesses; the R1 suite still runs | R1 §16, Phase T |

Everything else in R1 still applies (R2-SCOPE-02).

## 6. Where R2 refactors R1

1. **Owner data changes only through operations** applied to a merge state (§8.3). R1 records become projections written in the same transaction; validation moves into pure domain code that clients run too.
2. **Every Core-originated write to owner data goes through one authority path** (§8.3.5), including R1's ingestion, extraction, planning and rematching writes.
3. **The store records every change:** change-log entries and tombstones inside each transaction; a second read-only connection; streaming queries; savepoints; explicit disk-full errors (§8.15).
4. **Agenda inputs become portable** in `LisaAgendaInputs`.
5. **Delivery learns ownership,** firings and restore holds (§8.10).
6. **Devices get keys and a lifecycle** (§8.1); R1's legacy pairing stays for v1 devices.
7. **v1 becomes a translation layer** with durable caller-scoped parents (§8.12.2).
8. **Clients get a new core** (LisaClientCore, LisaReplicaStore, LisaSyncEngine, LisaSyncHTTP); LisaClient stays for v1 callers, queue import and the shared HTTP transport.
9. **Recovery evidence lives outside the database** as well (§8.4.4, §8.5), because a restore rewinds everything inside it. Writer fences and sender fences are recorded separately (§8.5.1, §8.10.6).
10. **Legacy requests have one converter** (`LisaLegacyRequests`, §8.12.2), used by the v1 translation and by queue imports; no device converts an R1 request.
11. **Provider integrations get one authorization module and their own adapters** (`LisaProviderAuth`, `LisaMicrosoftGraph`, `LisaGoogleAPIs`, `LisaCalDAV`, `LisaAnthropic`, `LisaOllama`, §8.19); R1's IMAP and SMTP adapters stay for iCloud and Proton, and R1's model adapter gains Structured Outputs.

## 7. Target architecture

### 7.1 Topology and authority (R2-ARCH-01, R2-ARCH-02)

- **Lisa Core**, one container on the owner's Linux server:
  - holds the canonical SQLite dataset, the operation ledger, the change log, snapshots, content blobs and the signed device roster;
  - keeps the recovery ledger and backup sets outside the database (§8.4.4);
  - runs the connectors, the scheduler and the outbox for email, calls and APNs;
  - serves v2 and the v1 translation over HTTPS on the VPN address, an owner listener on loopback, and the administrative CLI (§8.11.5);
  - holds every Core-only secret.
- **Each client** (Mac, iPhone, iPad) holds its replica, journal and device keys; runs local planning, briefs and search, nearby sync and relay, delegated and local notifications, optional inference, owner-requested public retrieval, SwiftUI and Siri; holds no Core secret.
- **Connections:** clients reach Core over the VPN with HTTPS and each other over authenticated nearby sessions; APNs and model providers are reached by outbound connections only.

### 7.2 Module plan

#### 7.2.1 New package modules

| Module | Responsibility | Direct dependencies | Linked by |
|---|---|---|---|
| `LisaSyncProtocol` | Wire types (§8.2, §8.4, §8.16): envelopes, receipts, coverage-bound change pages, paged-artifact headers, manifests, blobs, roster, lineage and confirmations, enrollment, reset and challenge messages, assignments and reports, frames; canonical encoder, strict decoder, size derivation, test vectors | Foundation | Core, clients |
| `LisaMerge` | Merge state and rules (§8.3), pieces, observed sets, authority, causal readiness, projection to R1 record shapes | LisaDomain, LisaTime, LisaSyncProtocol | Core, clients |
| `LisaTrust` | Signatures, token hashing, enrollment, reset and challenge proofs, roster and lineage verification, key history, head selection, nearby session layer | LisaCrypto, LisaSyncProtocol, LisaTime | Core, clients |
| `LisaAgendaInputs` | Portable planner and composer inputs, conflict-aware planning inputs (§8.3.4), offline intelligence | LisaDomain, LisaTime, LisaPlanning, LisaBriefComposer, LisaPolicy, LisaRanking | LisaAgenda, clients |
| `LisaFiringIdentity` (M3) | Occurrence IDs, firing numbers, the R1 key mapping, epoch-qualified generations and the slot key `(occurrence, firing, recipient)` used by holds, reservations and the submission journal (§8.10.1) | LisaDomain, LisaTime, LisaSyncProtocol | Core, clients |
| `LisaNotificationOwnership` (M8) | Device side of ownership: command storage, local schedule planning with the 32-request cap, OS-request intents and crash precedence, reconciliation (§8.10.2–§8.10.5) | LisaFiringIdentity, LisaDomain, LisaTime, LisaPolicy, LisaSyncProtocol | clients |
| `LisaLegacyRequests` (M3) | The one conversion of an R1 request into a parent and its complete set of Core-signed children, decided under R1's semantics; reply dependency index; terminal replies (§8.12.2) | LisaAPIContract, LisaSyncProtocol, LisaMerge, LisaCoreLedger | Core (LisaAPI, LisaCoreAPI) |
| `LisaArchive` | The protected archive format (§8.6.6): entries, HPKE sealing to several recipients, signatures, verification, admission re-checks | LisaSyncProtocol, LisaTrust, LisaCrypto | Core, clients, the post-downgrade tool |
| `LisaRecoveryLedger` | Append-only, hash-chained, Core-signed files outside the database: lineage and confirmations, trust records, writer- and sender-fence records, the submission journal with run starts and terminal checkpoints, backup index (§8.4.4, §8.5, §8.10.6) | LisaSyncProtocol, LisaTrust, LisaPorts | Core |
| `LisaCoreLedger` | Intake, duplicates, dependency release markers and paged edge expansion, atomic application, receipts, change log and interval coverage, snapshots, SQLite-resident blobs, retention and pruning, epochs, recovery state machine, delivery holds, the `AuthorityWriter` implementation | LisaStore, LisaPorts, LisaMerge, LisaTrust, LisaSyncProtocol, LisaFiringIdentity, LisaLogging | Core |
| `LisaCoreAPI` | The v2 router (§8.12.1) and the read-only migration lookups | LisaAPIContract, LisaCoreLedger, LisaApplication, LisaDevices, LisaPorts, LisaSyncProtocol | Core |
| `LisaMigration` | Preparation, fencing, forward migration, queue-import classification, rollback preflight, reverse migration, the post-downgrade archive workflow with its reconcile store (§8.14) | LisaStore, LisaMerge, LisaSyncProtocol, LisaArchive, LisaLegacyRequests | Core CLI |
| `LisaReplicaStore` | The client database (§8.6): replica, journal, drafts, receipts, local change journal and generation, partition cursors, staged installation, retention, blobs, export assembly | LisaSQLite, LisaDomain, LisaMerge, LisaSyncProtocol, LisaArchive | clients |
| `LisaLocalSearch` | Token index (implements `ReplicaIndexing`) | LisaSQLite, LisaDomain, LisaReplicaStore | clients |
| `LisaSyncEngine` | Reconciliation (§8.6.5), nearby sessions, relay, capabilities, backoff; declares `CoreTransport` and `PeerLink` | LisaSyncProtocol, LisaTrust, LisaMerge, LisaReplicaStore, LisaTime | clients |
| `LisaSyncHTTP` | The one production `CoreTransport`: LisaClient's `URLSessionTransport` (extended to `/v2/`) with an injected `ServerTrustEvaluator` | LisaSyncEngine, LisaClient | `Apps/Probe` and the apps (through LisaClientCore) |
| `LisaHTTP1` | Verified-destination HTTP/1.1 for public retrieval and configured model endpoints; the path-scoped selection and refusal rule (§8.9.3) | LisaHTTP | clients |
| `LisaFallbackInference` | Intent keys, reservations, the in-flight slot, uncertain sends, the five workflows (§8.9.2) | LisaInference, LisaOpenAICompatible, LisaReplicaStore, LisaHTTP | clients |

| `LisaProviderAuth` (M7) | OAuth 2.0 public-client authorization (code with PKCE, loopback redirect, pasted redirect), refresh and rotation, the encrypted provider-token store, revocation detection (§8.19.1) | LisaCrypto, LisaHTTP, LisaPorts, LisaTime | Core |
| `LisaMicrosoftGraph` (M7) | Outlook.com mail (message delta, raw MIME), sending (`sendMail`) and calendars (`calendarView` windows) through Microsoft Graph (§8.19.2) | LisaProviderAuth, LisaHTTP, LisaMIME, LisaICalendar, LisaPorts | Core |

#### 7.2.2 Changed and unchanged modules

- **Changed:** LisaDomain (pieces, provenance, owner reminders, suggestions, plan intents, algorithm version); LisaTime (`MonotonicClock` with boot time); LisaCrypto (P-256 signing and agreement, HKDF, HMAC, HPKE helpers over swift-crypto); LisaSQLite (streaming, savepoints, read connections, interrupt, incremental backup, checkpoints, disk-full errors); LisaStore (schema v5, change capture, tombstones that keep request keys, backup manifest v2 with digests); LisaOwnerData, LisaReview, LisaAgenda and LisaApplication (operations, `AuthorityWriter` calls); **LisaIngestion** (mail ingestion's direct proposal insert goes through `AuthorityWriter` in the connector's transaction, §8.3.5); LisaDelivery (ownership, firings, reservation serialization, restore holds, release); LisaDevices (enrollment, reset, roster, revocation, legacy links); LisaPlanning and LisaBriefComposer (algorithm version, conflict inputs, spoken uncertainty); LisaAPIContract (v2; v1 error mapping); LisaAPI (v1 translation through LisaLegacyRequests); LisaAPIServer (streamed size limits, chunk responses, explicit listeners); LisaHost (Core composition, health publishing, recovery mode); LisaRuntime (monotonic timing, run generation); LisaConfig (schema v2, `api.bindAddresses`, publish validation); LisaCLI (`lisa core`, `lisa archive`); LisaDemo (three-device scenario); LisaPresentation (rebuilt); LisaClient (`/v2/` paths in the transport, queue reading for import); **LisaOpenAICompatible** (Structured Outputs with `strict`, `max_completion_tokens`, sampling only where a model profile allows it, `X-Client-Request-Id`, §8.19.3); **LisaIMAP and LisaSMTP** (a pinned certificate for a local Bridge listener; no protocol change); **LisaTwilio** (read-only call-status reads, §8.19.4); **LisaIngestion** (the provider connectors as sources); **LisaConfig** (provider sections; the provider-token key reference).
- **Unchanged:** LisaRedaction, LisaSecrets, LisaLogging, LisaICalendar, LisaMIME, LisaXML (apart from any M0 fix), LisaFeeds, LisaHTMLText, LisaMatching, LisaRanking, LisaInference, LisaHTTP, LisaCurl, LisaHTTPSources, the other connectors and transport adapters (LisaCalendarFeeds kept for public calendars, news, X, Discord, APNs), LisaFixtures.
- **Apple-only (`Apps/`):**
  - **Extended R1 folders:** `Shared/Trust` (SPKI pins, trust modes and chain bundles, §8.1.2), `Shared/Keychain` (device keys, Core credential, enrollment retry item, export key), `Shared/Notifications` (ownership and reconciliation), `Shared/Storage` (replica directory, backup exclusion).
  - **New folders:** `Shared/Nearby` (after N-1), `Shared/Background`, `Shared/PublicFetch` (after E-1 for production use), `Shared/Import` (old-queue import), `Shared/Export` (save panels and document pickers), `Shared/Pairing` (text entry, CoreImage rendering, VisionKit scanning).
  - **Proof and probe apps:** `Apps/Proof` (X1) and `Apps/Probe` (X2). Neither ships.
  - **Owner verification:** `Apps/OwnerVerification/` holds the XCUITest targets and session test plans that drive the actual apps (§10.8). It doesn't ship.

#### 7.2.3 Ports at the platform and service boundaries

| Port | Declared in | Implemented by | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ReplicaSigning` | LisaReplicaStore | `Apps/Shared/Keychain` (Secure Enclave or Keychain key); software key in harnesses | Signs inside the local-change transaction; reports "key unavailable" without failing the save (§8.6.2) |
| `ReplicaIndexing` | LisaReplicaStore | LisaLocalSearch | Index updates in the same transaction, including staged indexes (§8.6.3) |
| `IdentifierHashing` | LisaFiringIdentity | LisaCrypto SHA-256 via the composition root | Occurrence and OS request identifiers |
| `CoreTransport` | LisaSyncEngine | LisaSyncHTTP (production, Apple and Linux loopback); a test-only libcurl pinning transport (harness) | Direct HTTPS sync |
| `ServerTrustEvaluator` | LisaClient (R1) | `Apps/Shared/Trust` | Core pin and anchor checks |
| `PeerLink` | LisaSyncEngine | `Apps/Shared/Nearby`; in-memory link labeled simulated | Nearby sessions |
| `DestinationResolver`, `DestinationConnector` | LisaHTTP1 | `Apps/Shared/PublicFetch` (`dns_sd` resolution and RFC 7050 discovery scoped to one interface, `NWConnection` to an IP endpoint pinned to that interface); test resolver and byte stream | Resolution and connection to checked addresses only, on one path (§8.9.3) |
| `LocalNotificationCenter` | LisaNotificationOwnership | `Apps/Shared/Notifications`; simulated center | Scheduling and reconciliation |
| `KeyStore` | LisaTrust | `Apps/Shared/Keychain`; file-backed software keys (Core, harness) | Device and Core keys |
| `ArchiveDestination` | LisaClientCore | `Apps/Shared/Export` (save panel, document picker); a directory (harness) | Writable export outside the container (§8.6.6) |
| `R1RequestEvidenceReading` | LisaPorts | LisaStore | Read-only v1 lookups for migration (§8.14.3) |
| `AuthorityWriter` | LisaPorts | LisaCoreLedger | The only write path for source, model and system authority (§8.3.5) |
| `RecoveryLedgerWriting` | LisaPorts | LisaRecoveryLedger | Out-of-database records (§8.4.4) |
| `SubmissionJournal` | LisaPorts | LisaRecoveryLedger | Records each external submission's start before the provider call and its outcome after, run starts, heartbeats and terminal checkpoints (§8.10.6) |
| `HealthPublishing` | LisaRuntime | LisaHost (`run/health.json`) | One-shot probes without opening the database (§8.11.4) |
| `MonotonicClock` | LisaTime | `CLOCK_BOOTTIME`/continuous clock; manual clock in tests | Timeouts, backoff, freshness |
| `ProviderTokenStore` | LisaProviderAuth | LisaHost: encrypted files on the data volume, keyed by the `provider-token-key` secret file; an in-memory store in harnesses | Rotating OAuth refresh tokens, which read-only secret files can't hold (§8.19.1) |
| `MailSource`, `CalendarSource`, `MailSender` | LisaPorts | LisaMicrosoftGraph, LisaGoogleAPIs, LisaIMAP, LisaCalDAV, LisaCalendarFeeds, LisaSMTP | One interface per capability across providers (§8.19.2) |
| `ModelProvider` (R1) | LisaInference | LisaOpenAICompatible, LisaAnthropic, LisaOllama; R1's deterministic provider | Core inference and the Apple fallback (§8.19.3) |

### 7.3 Platform separation (R2-ARCH-03)

- **Core-only:** LisaStore, LisaIngestion, LisaReview, LisaAgenda, LisaDelivery, LisaDevices, LisaOwnerData, LisaApplication, LisaRuntime, LisaAPI, LisaAPIServer, LisaHost, LisaCoreLedger, LisaCoreAPI, LisaRecoveryLedger, LisaLegacyRequests, LisaMigration, LisaProviderAuth, LisaMicrosoftGraph, LisaGoogleAPIs, LisaCalDAV, LisaCurl, every adapter except the three model adapters (LisaOpenAICompatible, LisaAnthropic, LisaOllama), LisaCLI.
- **Harness-only:** the owner-harness targets (§10.8), which no product links.
- **Client closure:** LisaClientCore and its dependencies, with no libcurl, NIO or Core module. A temporary check fails verification if any client product's closure contains a Core-only target.
- **Apple-only code** stays in `Apps/`.

### 7.4 Required behavior, mechanism, decision and experiment

Every design in §8 separates four kinds of statement:
- **Required** — what `specs/R2.md` or an inherited R1 requirement demands, with its ID.
- **Mechanism** — the implementation chosen here; replaceable by an equivalent that meets the same checks.
- **Decision** — something the owner settled (O-1–O-11, P-01–P-08), recorded in §16.
- **Experiment** — something still to be established on hardware or a host (N-1, E-1–E-12, §10.5); work that depends on it is marked.

## 8. Designs

Each design becomes a `docs/DECISIONS.md` entry from D-53 on. Proposed checks are written in the future tense; the scenario IDs (S-nn) are defined in §20–§21 and recorded in `docs/VERIFICATION-SCENARIOS.md`.

### 8.1 Identity, enrollment and the trust lifecycle (R2-TRUST-01–05; finding 4, finding 5; trust lifecycle)

#### 8.1.1 Identities

- **Dataset:** a random 128-bit `datasetID`, created by `lisa core init` or by `lisa core migrate prepare` (§8.14.1), which records it before cutover.
- **Recovery epochs:** a random 128-bit epoch ID with an epoch record in the lineage (§8.5.2).
- **Core keys** (secret files, §8.11.2):
  - the signing key (P-256) with a key history of `{key, validFromPosition, retiredAtPosition}`;
  - the export key (P-256 key agreement), whose public half the roster carries and which opens protected archives (§8.6.6), with retired keys kept;
  - the TLS key and certificate.
- **Device:** a `deviceID` assigned at enrollment; an operation-signing key; a nearby transport key (agreement key for Design A, TLS key for Design B); a device export key; a device-generated 256-bit Core credential of which Core stores only the SHA-256 verifier; the trust anchor from pairing. Keys live in the Secure Enclave where available, otherwise in Keychain items available after first unlock on this device only.
- **Incarnations:** every launch that opens the replica starts a new random 128-bit incarnation with sequence numbers from 1; its first operation names the previous incarnation and its last sequence number. A restored device copy therefore never repeats an identity: the next launch is a new incarnation and the chain shows the fork.

#### 8.1.2 Enrollment and the trust bootstrap (finding 4 fixed; finding 5 preserved)

**Required:** single-use, owner-controlled pairing that binds the intended Core identity; an authenticated Core path; no certificate acceptance outside explicit pins or private CAs (R2-TRUST-01, -02).

**Mechanism**
1. **Token.** `lisa core device enroll --name … --class iphone|ipad|mac` stores an enrollment record: random 128-bit ID, token hash, 10-minute expiry, attempt counter, state `open`, intended name and class, intended host name. The token is 32 random bytes; Core never stores or logs it.
   - One function hashes it at issuance and verification: `tokenHash = SHA-256(ASCII "lisa-enroll-token-v1" ‖ 0x00 ‖ token)`. The payload carries the token as unpadded base64url; Core decodes it, refuses any length other than 32, then hashes.
