### 10.3 The R1 suite (O-5, P-07)

- **At M0** the original suite runs unchanged, first in debug and then in release, and its outcomes are recorded as they are. Narrow production fixes follow, one per commit, then a rerun. Later runs: M3, M5, M8, M9.
- **Each failure is classified** as an implementation regression (fixed in the implementation), an explicitly replaced requirement (reported as "replaced by <R2 ID>", test unchanged), or an environment-specific issue (cause and evidence reported).
- **Changing a test or test-support file** needs a request naming the file, the reason and the evidence. The original outcome stays in the record regardless.
- **Source compatibility kept for the suite:**
  - LisaHost's `application`, `close`, `deviceTransport`, `runtime`, `recorder`, `ownerTransport`, `storage`, `recoveryNotes`, `send`, `router`, `importDirectory`, `registerLocalInstallation`, `ownerSettings`, `logger` and `backup`;
  - the facade's commands;
  - LisaClient's typed client, `OfflineQueue`, `URLSessionTransport`, `HostEndpoint` and `NotificationActionHandler`.

  The local-notification installation stays as an optional host capability, because 17 test files use it.

### 10.4 Controlled substitutes (test-only, labeled)

- **From R1:** the scripted HTTP transport; loopback TLS servers with run-time certificates; scripted IMAP, SMTP and APNs servers; synthetic keys and devices.
- **New for R2, in the temporary harnesses:**
  - the in-memory peer link;
  - loopback OpenAI-, Anthropic- and Ollama-shaped servers;
  - the libcurl pinning transport;
  - a response-dropping relay, and a relay that captures the exact bytes it forwards (S-03);
  - the test resolver and byte stream for destinations, including a resolver that synthesizes AAAA answers for chosen names only;
  - the simulated notification center, with an injectable crash between the OS add and the recorded success, and an adjustable wall clock;
  - a scripted device client for M3 assignments, reports and local scheduling (replaced by the real device side at M8);
  - a harness network that partitions one Core host from another while a client still reaches it, and lets a scheduler and a local command run on the cut-off host (S-10);
  - scripted Twilio and SMTP accept-after-cut servers, and a journal-writer that leaves a claim open at the cut (S-11);
  - a clock offset for the frozen R1 host, to run past its 30-day ledger retention, and a read-only reader of R1's database (S-02);
  - a trust-bundle builder that produces valid, tampered, foreign-dataset and incomplete bundles (S-10);
  - a partition-repair driver that delays snapshots and injects pages during a repair (S-08);
  - controlled Graph, Gmail API, Calendar API and CalDAV servers, IMAP and SMTP servers with a self-signed certificate (as Bridge's), and a Twilio-shaped server with call-status progression (S-21, S-23);
  - **the controlled local Ollama server:** the `ollama/ollama` image, pinned by digest, with a small public model pulled into it, run in the rootless engine with no account. It exercises Ollama's real protocol and is reported as a controlled real-protocol server, never as the owner's deployment (S-22);
  - container `tmpfs` exhaustion and `max_page_count`;
  - a pinned-reader driver;
  - a wall-clock offset for clock-step tests;
  - run-time CAs and intermediates;
  - frozen R1 binaries, client and fixtures;
  - synthetic Mac, iPhone and iPad replicas.
- **Limits:** none contacts a live service, Apple, a model provider or the owner's network. The owner-verification harness carries its own controlled servers and fixtures, written separately (§10.8).

### 10.5 Deciding experiments

| ID | Question | Experiment and success criterion | Who and when | Gates |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| N-1 | Nearby transport security | Both designs on Mac–iPhone, Mac–iPad and iPhone–iPad, on shared Wi-Fi, without an access point and without Internet. Success means: correct peer identification; refusal of an unenrolled identity; an exporter-bound hello; refusal of a wrong binding; reconnection after kill and Wi-Fi toggle; deduplication; 100 frames timed; TLS version and suite recorded; permission prompt and denial recorded. Design A also needs a forward-secret ECDHE-PSK suite on every pair. Design B uses proof-only certificates | Owner, X1 (harness test plan `X1`) | Production nearby transport (M6). **A release requirement:** if both designs fail, the gates stay open, a compliant redesign is planned and the proofs repeat |
| E-1 | Public fetch on Apple with Core unavailable | The X1 fetch probe (§8.9.3), including dual-stack, NAT64, an independently translated destination and split-VPN paths, and interface-scoped `dns_sd` resolution and discovery with `requiredInterface` | Owner, X1 | Production retrieval design (M7). **A release requirement:** a failing platform keeps its gates open; a compliant redesign is planned and proved there |
| E-2 | Apple SDK module maps and swift-crypto | Build the portable modules for macOS, iOS and iPadOS | Owner, X1 | Apple builds |
| E-3 | Production transport and enrollment on a device | `Apps/Probe` with LisaSyncHTTP, chain matrix | Owner, X2 | — |
| E-4 | First rootless container run | M2 harness, then the M4 image | Claude | — |
| E-5 | libxml2 2.15 and OpenSSL 4.0 compatibility | M0 build and suite | Claude | — |
| E-6 | `BGTaskScheduler` grants | Observed at X4 and X6 | Owner | — |
| E-7 | Local notification behavior | X6 | Owner | — |
| E-8 | Production retrieval and inference on all three devices | X5 | Owner | Apple gate |
| E-9 | SELinux confinement | Target host | Owner | Container gate |
| E-10 | Sandboxed Mac: Keychain, listening, signing, export, import | X3, again at X7 | Owner | Apple gate |
| E-11 | swift-certificates on devices (if Design B and P-08) | X4 | Owner | Apple gate |
| E-12 | An R1 Mac build from `491c90a` | Owner builds it at or before X3 | Owner | X7's genuine-app part |
| E-13 | Can Graph read a calendar one personal Microsoft account shares with another? (Microsoft doesn't document it, §1.5) | LS-1: the owner shares a test calendar from a personal account with the dedicated account, which accepts it; `OV-CAL-OUTLOOK-01` lists it and reads its window | Owner, LS-1 | Outlook.com calendar row. If it fails, the row stays open, and the only other authenticated route — authorizing the owner's own account with `Calendars.Read` — comes to the owner as a decision, because it reaches the owner's personal account |
| E-14 | Does iCloud answer CalDAV with an app-specific password at `caldav.icloud.com`, and does a calendar shared read-only to the dedicated Apple Account appear there? (Apple documents neither, §1.5) | LS-1: `OV-CAL-ICLOUD-01` discovers the principal and lists the shared calendar | Owner, LS-1 | iCloud Calendar row; if it fails, the row stays open with the evidence |
| E-15 | Does Proton Mail Bridge run headless in a sidecar: login, password store, restart, certificate export? | LS-1, only if P-09 is granted | Owner (login) and Claude (sidecar definition) | Proton Mail row |

### 10.6 Owner-run Apple sessions and live sessions

At each session the owner records, through the owner harness (§10.8): date; device models and OS versions; Xcode version; signing team and provisioning availability (including Push where relevant); permission states; and each result with its status, executor and cause, plus logs. Dates and results stay pending until then. Device availability is not verification: only a completed owner-run case supplies a physical or live result. Hardware supplies no APNs credentials, live authorization or production host.

| X2 | M3 | `Apps/Probe`: a small signed app with LisaSyncEngine, LisaReplicaStore, LisaTrust and **LisaSyncHTTP**; test plan `X2` | iPhone and Mac (iPad optional), with a Core the owner runs from the M3 build | E-3: the chain matrix with leaf pinning; on every trust failure zero enrollment POSTs and no token disclosure (at most the token-free anchors GET); the Keychain retry item; a durable edit, sync, receipt lookup, camera denial |
| X3 | M5 | The full apps; test plan `X3` | Mac, iPhone, iPad | R2-A01, A02, A15; E-10; backup exclusion; export outside the container; the icon crop; E-12 (the R1 Mac build) |
| X4 | M6 | The full apps with the production nearby transport; test plan `X4` | All three pairs | R2-A03–A07; E-6; E-11 if applicable |
| X5 | M7 | The full apps with retrieval and inference; test plan `X5` | **Mac, iPhone and iPad**, Core unavailable, controlled endpoints; the owner's own OpenAI and Anthropic keys and Ollama server for the live parts | E-8: permitted public GET (production path), disallowed destination refused before connection, AAAA-only name deferred, path change, redirects, the owner's split-tunnel VPN connected, authenticated model POST for each provider shape; the verified Core-unavailable case per platform |
| X6 | M8 | The full apps with notifications and Siri; test plan `X6` with the APNs preflight | iPhone, iPad, Mac; the owner's Apple Developer account, used only by the owner | R2-A09–A14; E-7; APNs live parts (§10.9) |

### 10.7 Containers

The authorized rootless engine on this host is used from M2. The owner's server provides target-host evidence. Every result names the engine, context, host, architecture and image digests. arm64 is `NOT_RUN_ENVIRONMENT`.

### 10.8 The owner-verification harness (O-6 extension, owner decision of 2026-11-22)

**What it is.** A tracked, versioned, comprehensive, package-level harness with which the owner verifies the Apple apps and the named services under the owner's own identity. It is a deliverable in its own right, authored separately from the temporary implementation harnesses (`CONSTRICTION.md`'s test rules; R2-DEV-02 "unless separately authorized"): it is written from this plan and the product's public interfaces, uses no file, code or data from `.tmp/`, and works from a clean checkout without private developer-machine state and without Claude operating any owner account. Ordinary development, sync, migration and recovery harnesses stay temporary in `.tmp/r2/<run-id>/` (O-6).

**Locations and entry points**
- **`Harness/`** (tracked):
  - `README.md`: the quick start on one page — prerequisites, `swift build --product lisa-verify`, `lisa-verify quickstart` (creates `~/LisaVerify/config.json5` from the example, runs the preflight and lists the cases), a first fixture run, a first owner-live run;
  - `Sources/LisaVerifyKit/`: the case model (ID, area, provider, interface, capability, environment, mode, required result level, expected outcome, manual steps), the runner, the resumable run store, the result schema, reports, the redaction allowlist and the preflight checks;
  - `Sources/LisaVerifyCases/`: the case catalogue, IDs `OV-<AREA>-<PROVIDER>-<NN>` (`OV-MAIL-GMAIL-07`, `OV-CAL-ICLOUD-12`, `OV-AI-OLLAMA-05`, `OV-CALL-TWILIO-03`, `OV-APPLE-X6-14`, `OV-HOST-04`);
  - `Sources/LisaVerifyServers/`: the harness's own controlled provider-shaped servers for fixture mode — Graph, Gmail API, Calendar API, CalDAV, IMAP and SMTP with a Bridge-like self-signed certificate, OpenAI, Anthropic, Ollama, Twilio and APNs — labeled controlled and never contacting a service;
  - `Sources/lisa-verify/`: the executable;
  - `Fixtures/`: synthetic mail (MIME variants, attachments, duplicates, encoded headers), calendars (zones and DST changes, recurrence with moved and cancelled instances, all-day and multi-day events), model transcripts (valid, invalid, refusal, truncated, usage variants) and Twilio responses, generated by `lisa-verify fixtures generate --seed <n>` with recorded digests; no real personal data;
  - `compose/controlled.yaml`: the controlled servers and the controlled local Ollama container for the rootless engine;
  - `config/owner-verify.example.json5`: every platform and provider section with placeholders such as `<dedicated Gmail address>`, `file:<path to the app-specific password>`, `keychain:<item name>` — never a secret;
  - `docs/`: `SETUP-APPLE.md` (Xcode, XcodeGen as R1 uses it, the team ID in the git-ignored `Apps/Config/Signing.local.xcconfig`, device registration, entitlements, profiles, the APNs key and token setup), one setup guide per provider (`SETUP-OUTLOOK.md`, `SETUP-GMAIL.md`, `SETUP-ICLOUD.md`, `SETUP-PROTON.md`, `SETUP-OPENAI.md`, `SETUP-ANTHROPIC.md`, `SETUP-OLLAMA.md`, `SETUP-TWILIO.md`) with account tier, client or bridge requirements, required scopes, credential custody, limitations, propagation delays and the testable access path; `CASES.md` (generated from the catalogue, with each case's expected outcome and manual steps); `RESULTS.md` (the schema); `CLEANUP.md`.
- **`Apps/OwnerVerification/`** (tracked): XCUITest targets `LisaOwnerUITests-macOS` and `LisaOwnerUITests-iOS` (iPhone and iPad) that drive **the actual Lisa apps and their production code paths** through their UI, and one test plan per session (`X1`–`X7`) and area, added to `Apps/project.yml`. They run with `xcodebuild test -scheme <scheme> -testPlan <plan> -destination 'platform=iOS,id=<UDID>'`, or from Xcode. Steps no test can drive — radios, Focus, the lock screen, permission prompts, physical placement, hearing a call — are guided by `lisa-verify guide <case>` on the Mac, which shows each step with its expected outcome and records the owner's observation. App-side evidence comes from the production Diagnostics export (IDs, counters and states only).
- **Build dependencies:** the harness targets are declared in the root `Package.swift` with `path: "Harness/Sources/…"`, use only Foundation and the approved swift-argument-parser, SwiftNIO and swift-crypto, link the product modules they exercise, and are linked by no product; `Package.resolved` doesn't change. The Apple parts use Xcode, XCTest and XcodeGen, which the owner already uses for R1's apps.

**Modes**
- **fixture** (default): deterministic fixtures and controlled servers; no account; runs on Fedora (Claude) and on the owner's Mac.
- **device:** owner-invoked; Apple builds on physical devices, signed and provisioned by the owner.
- **live:** owner-invoked; real providers under the owner's identity. Before running, it lists every real effect the selected cases will cause — each message and its recipient, each notification, each model request with its estimated charge, each call and its number — and needs `--confirm-real-actions <run-id>`. It refuses any recipient or number outside the owner's approved test lists.

**Commands**
- `lisa-verify preflight apple|<provider>|all`: for Apple, the Xcode version, SDKs, signing identities (summarized, never exported), profiles for each bundle ID with team, entitlements and `aps-environment` (development or production), connected devices, the APNs key reference (a path, never the key), the topic matching the bundle ID, and Core's APNs environment matching the profile — it states which profile will run; for a provider, placeholders replaced, the dedicated account's prerequisites, the token store or password reference present, and, in live mode only, one read-only authentication check shown before it runs.
- `run --case <id> | --area <area> | --session <X1…X7 | LS-1…LS-3> | --matrix full [--mode fixture|device|live]`; `resume <run-id>`; `retry <run-id> <case>`; `guide <case>`; `observe <run-id> <case> --status pass|fail --observed "…"`; `import-diagnostics <run-id> <file>`; `report <run-id>`; `export <run-id> --redacted <file>`; `cleanup <run-id>`; `revoke-local <provider>`.
- **Unique IDs:** run IDs `run-<UTC time>-<6 base32 characters>`. Every synthetic resource a run asks the owner to create — calendar names and event titles, message subjects, prompts — carries `LISA-VERIFY <run-id> <case>`, so nothing collides with the owner's data, and cleanup finds only what the run made.
- **Repeatable and resumable:** the run store (`~/LisaVerify/runs/<run-id>/run.json`) records each case's state; a run resumes after a crash or a pause; a single failed case reruns with `retry`.

**Results**
- `results.jsonl`, one record per case: run ID, case ID, commit, harness version, provider and interface (for example "Gmail API", "Graph", "CalDAV", "IMAP via Bridge"), non-secret configuration (a digest plus allowlisted fields), platform, device model and OS version, Xcode version, APNs environment and profile kind, model ID where relevant, assumptions, expected result, observed result, result level (§10.1), status, executor and exact cause, times, and evidence file names.
- `summary.md`, owner-readable: per session and area, what passed, failed or waits, with causes and next steps.
- Manual observations, partial runs and single retries are recorded like any other result.
- **Redacted export:** `export --redacted` writes allowlisted fields only. It never contains service tokens, credentials, calendar URLs, mail content, prompts containing owner data, telephone numbers, provisioning material or raw push tokens; addresses and numbers become stable per-run aliases. Planted canaries check the export (S-24).
- Results stay on the owner's machine (`~/LisaVerify/`), never in the repository. The owner shares whichever redacted exports they choose; Claude evaluates those, and only redacted evidence is attached to the project's reports.

**Owner resources, cleanup and revocation**
- Each setup guide lists the dedicated account to create, the scopes or passwords to grant, the test calendar to create in the provider's own UI and share read-only, the approved test recipient and number, and the propagation delays to expect.
- `cleanup <run-id>` lists what the run created and removes what Lisa itself holds (local token files, run data). Lisa's integrations are read-only, so provider-side items — test messages in the dedicated mailbox, the test calendar and its events — are removed by the owner in the provider's UI following `CLEANUP.md`, which names only `LISA-VERIFY <run-id>` items. `revoke-local` deletes the harness's local credentials, and each guide links the provider's page for revoking the grant. Nothing unrelated is touched.
- **Faults that can't be induced safely or reliably against a real service** — token expiry after six months, provider outages, a 529, a rate-limit storm, a Bridge crash mid-fetch, a Twilio timeout after acceptance — run only against controlled servers, carry the Simulated class, and are never presented as live results.

|---|---|---|
| M1 | Core of `Harness/` (kit, CLI, modes, schema, reports, redaction, Apple preflight), `Apps/OwnerVerification` skeleton and test plan `X1`, quick start, `SETUP-APPLE.md` | X1 |
| M3 | Test plan `X2`; sync and enrollment cases | X2 |
| M4 | `OV-HOST-*` cases for the target host | Target-host evidence |
| M5 | Test plan `X3` | X3 |
| M6 | Test plan `X4` | X4 |
| M7 | Test plan `X5`; provider cases, controlled servers and setup guides for the eight mail and calendar combinations and the three model providers; LS-1 and LS-2 guides | X5, LS-1, LS-2 |

**Acceptance (S-24, every milestone from M1):** a fresh clone builds the harness and runs every fixture case without network access to any provider; a run interrupted mid-case resumes; one failed case reruns alone; a manual observation is recorded; the redacted export contains none of the canaries planted in configuration, prompts, mail, calendar URLs, numbers and push tokens; `cleanup` removes only what the run created; a delivery checklist confirms that nothing was taken from `.tmp/`.

### 10.9 Acceptance matrices: what "fully tested" means for the Apple apps and the named services

A required row is **closed** only when every case it names has the result level it requires, on every platform, pair or provider it names (§10.1). Until then it is open, with each pending case's status, executor and cause. Disabling an integration by default, or having no credential, never removes its row. A documented provider limitation keeps its row open.

**Apple apps, by requirement, platform, pair and physical condition** (owner-run; harness test plans; required result `owner-verified` unless stated)

| R2-A01 builds | X1 (portable), X3 (apps) | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | — | Xcode version, schemes, a build log per platform |
| R2-A02 capture, edit, plan, brief with Core unreachable | X3 | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | Core stopped | Each action saved locally and later reconciled |

| R2-A04 nearby without Internet; no access point | X1 (N-1), X4 | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | Every pair with the Internet disconnected, and every pair with no access point | Per pair and condition, as above |

**X6 in detail (local and remote notifications).** Cases cover: local notifications with Core offline; APNs registration; the app, topic and environment matching; token changes; foreground, background, locked and relevant terminated states; permission changes; Focus; deduplication and recovery; and a synthetic remote push the owner triggers. Development and production APNs profiles both run where applicable, and each result names the profile that ran. Five facts are recorded separately: provider acceptance (APNs 200 and `apns-id`), device receipt (the app's callback or the delivered list), OS presentation or suppression (the owner's observation), the user's action, and reconciliation (Core's state afterwards).

## 13. Commits and buildable checkpoints

- **One file-level operation per commit,** staging only that file. Implementation and documentation are committed separately; messages are `add: …`, `change: …` or `remove: …`. `git status` is checked before every commit, and changes Claude didn't make are never staged.
- **Ordering keeps the tree buildable:** new files before their callers, additive changes before removals, protocol additions with default behavior before conformances.
- **When a change can't be ordered that way** (a signature change touching several files), the commits form a span:
  - the span's last commit is the **buildable checkpoint**, built and tested on the working tree before the next span starts;
  - `R2-CHECKPOINT.md` records the span's first and last commit and the checkpoint's HEAD with the build result.
  - Earlier commits are never checked out to test them, because checkouts aren't permitted (§3).

## 14. Checkpoint and resume (R2-DEV-03; O-7)

- **The record:** a tracked `R2-CHECKPOINT.md`, beginning at M0. It holds:
  - branch and baseline; current milestone; completed requirement IDs;
  - modified and uncommitted files;
  - commands and results, with evidence locations;
  - open conflicts and blockers; pending sessions and experiments;
  - permissions in force;
  - the last buildable checkpoint;
  - the next steps.
- **Updates:** at every milestone boundary and before any pause. Nothing is reverted to look clean.
- **On resume:** the actual files and processes are inspected first.

## 17. Risks

- **Scale.** Much of the Apple code can only be syntax-checked here. Mitigation: portable logic first, thin adapters, early X1–X3, the shared transport proved at X2, and owner-harness runners delivered before each session.
- **Nearby security.** Design A may fail N-1; Design B needs P-08 for production certificates. N-1 is a release requirement, so a failure of both designs means a redesign and repeated proofs, with the gates open meanwhile.
- **Apple retrieval** may fail E-1 on a platform. E-1 is a release requirement: the client defers to Core as a runtime fallback, the gates stay open, and a compliant redesign is proved. Names with only AAAA records are always deferred to Core on Apple devices (§8.9.3).
- **Rootless versus target host.** Networking, UID mapping and SELinux differ, so those checks repeat on the target.
- **Fedora drift:** libxml2 2.15, OpenSSL 4.0 (E-5), Swift 6.3.2 versus 6.4.2.
- **Recovery depends on owner actions:** stopping or fencing another host, keeping off-host copies of the recovery ledger and backups, backing up secret files, and collecting trust bundles from devices whose keys changed after a backup. The documentation and the restore review ask for each.
- **Owner attestations are trusted as stated.** A writer fence attested as stopped can't be verified by a Core that can't reach the old host. Mitigation: a documented procedure that disables every restart; `fence --local` whenever the old host is reachable, which also binds the journal's end and exports the tail; the attestation text kept in the ledger. Client isolation is no longer accepted as a fence.
- **A lost or merely attested volume leaves deliveries held.** Without a fence that binds the old writer's final journal entry, every slot it might have sent stays held until the owner releases it or it expires (§8.10.6).
- **Reconciling a lost task create needs the owner** whenever R1's own ledger record is gone; R1 offers no other identity for a task (§8.14.8).
- **Provider access can change.** Google's Personal user type, Microsoft's directory requirement for app registrations, Apple's undocumented CalDAV endpoint and the Graph route for shared personal calendars (E-13, E-14) are outside Lisa's control. Mitigation: each is checked by the owner at LS-1 and documented in its setup guide; a failure keeps its row open and, where another route would change the trust model, comes to the owner.
- **Proton:** Proton Calendar has no compliant route at all, and Proton Mail needs a new component (P-09) that runs only on 64-bit x86 Linux. Both rows stay open until the owner decides (§22).
- **Model APIs change quickly:** sampling parameters dropped by newer models, Anthropic's dated API versions, new structured-output rules. Mitigation: the version and model profile are configuration, adapters reject unknown answers as invalid rather than guessing, and the owner's LS-2 session re-verifies against the current services.
- **The owner harness must stay honest as the product changes.** Mitigation: it builds from a clean checkout at every milestone, its fixture mode runs in CI-like fashion on Fedora, and its redaction is tested with canaries (S-24).
- **Blobs inside SQLite** grow the database and its backups; staging, promotion and WAL growth are bounded per transaction (§8.16.3) and measured at M2 and M3.
- **Inherent limits, documented:**
  - revocation reaches a device only when it learns of it;
  - an isolated device's only copy can be lost;
  - exactly-once delivery is impossible, and released holds can duplicate;
  - handoffs and the 32-request cap can leave coverage holes;
  - RFC 7050 can't learn range-specific prefixes, so AAAA answers are never used on Apple devices;
  - per-device model budgets aren't a global cap;
  - R1 has no conditional preference confirm or delete, so the post-downgrade tool can only detect, not prevent, a change made between its read and R1's commit.
- **Migration** is verified on synthetic data only. R1's Apple apps may not build (E-12).
- **Owner availability:** X1–X7 and LS-1–LS-3 need the owner's time; N-1 and E-1 also gate production designs.

## 18. Stopping conditions

Work on an affected item stops only when:
- an action needs authorization outside the approved scope;
- an enforced platform restriction needs the owner;
- a concrete technical blocker can't be resolved within scope;
- credentials, Apple sessions, the target host or live validation are needed — the owner's to run, never Claude's;
- a provider lacks a capability a requirement needs, which is reported with primary-source evidence and the affected gate (§22);
- a deciding experiment hasn't run where its result is a stated prerequisite.

Independent work continues, and each blocked operation is reported with its exact cause.

## 19. End-to-end acceptance narratives

Both narratives are proposed checks. They run as portable harnesses with synthetic data, scripted providers and, before M8, a scripted device client: N-A at M3, its container part at M4, and its delegated-firing step again at M8 with the real device side; N-B at M9, with device parts at X7. Each step lists the stage, the evidence available, what is allowed, and what the owner sees. Every component a step uses exists at the milestone that runs it (§9).

### 19.1 Sibling restores, a previous writer cut off but still writing, a rotated key, a device key Core doesn't know, a newer revocation, shared data and provider acceptances (N-A)

**Setup.** Epoch E1 runs under Core key K1 on host H1, with a Mac, an iPhone, an iPad and a fourth device C enrolled, and `retainRestorable = 2`. Then:
1. Backup A is taken and validated with the key-backup anchor; its manifest records `s_cut(A)`.
2. Core rotates K1→K2 (recorded in the database and the recovery ledger). The owner saves K2's private half to the offline key backup, as `docs/SECURITY.md` asks.
3. The owner revokes C with a K2-signed roster.
4. The iPhone commits edit X. A large capture Y1 commits with blob b. Core delegates firing 1 of reminder P to the iPhone, which schedules it locally.
5. Backup B is taken; R = {A, B}.
6. The owner copies the backups and the recovery ledger, with the journal as it stands, off-host.
7. The iPad rotates its device key (D1→D2); Core accepts the key update and publishes roster v9 (K2) naming D2. The iPad then goes offline and saves edit Y2, which references blob b. Only H1's volume and the iPad hold v9.
8. The scripted SMTP server accepts an email reminder for slot O (firing 1). H1's journal records it; the off-host copy doesn't.
9. H1 fails and is lost with its volume.

| # | Step and stage | Evidence available | Allowed action | What the owner sees |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Restore on host H2 (stage 0) | A's manifest, anchored by the key backup; B's off-host copy fails validation; the off-host ledger copy holds K1→K2, C's revocation and B's index; the key backup holds K2's private half | `lisa core restore A` creates epoch E2; `trust import-key` loads K2, accepted because its public half matches the newest key in the merged history | "Restoring backup A. Backup B failed validation (digest mismatch) and is not used. Signing key K2 loaded from your key backup." |
| 2 | Fence H1 (stage 0 → 1) | H1 and its disks are destroyed | The owner follows the documented procedure and attests `owner-attested-stopped` (volume destroyed). Sender fences become `writer-stopped` at that time. No fence binds H1's journal | "Previous Lisa Core on H1: stopped (attested by you, <time>); its journal can't be checked. Delivery suspended." |
| 3 | Evidence collection (stage 1) | iPhone and Mac authenticate by challenge with keys from A's roster and upload K2-signed rosters, the K1→K2 record, operation inventories (X, Y1) and assignment inventories (P scheduled on the iPhone). C's upload is quarantined once the merged evidence revokes it. The iPad is offline | Collect; nothing applied | `trust review`: "Device C: revoked (<time>, K2). iPad: not yet reported. Mac, iPhone: active. Did any device enroll or change its key after <A's time>? Collect its trust records before confirming." |
| 4 | Confirm (stage 2) | H1's writer fence is closed; no trust bundle is pending | `trust confirm` writes confirmation n+1 for E2, signed with K2. Devices accept E2 only now (a confirmed head that descends from E1 at A's cut) and refresh their credentials | "Membership confirmed; ordinary work admitted next." |
| 5 | Admit (stage 3) | iPhone and Mac bodies for X and Y1, retained because A doesn't contain them; blob b, because Y1 holds it | Replay in dependency order; X and Y1 accepted | "Replayed 2 changes. Not yet heard from: iPad." |
| 6 | Discover B | A good copy of B turns up; `backup scan` validates it against the anchored history | `restore` is not repeated. B's operations after A's cut are imported as replay candidates and deduplicate against X and Y1 | "Backup B found: 2 operations available, 2 already applied." |
| 7 | Second restore while H2 runs, cut off from clients (stage 0 → 1 on H3) | H2's volume is misconfigured, so the owner restores A again on H3 (E3). The Mac committed edit Z on H2 earlier and holds H2's receipt. The owner removes H2's VPN route (`fence note --client-isolated`). H2 keeps running: at 07:00 its scheduler recomputes the plan and syncs reminders, and at 07:05 a script the owner left on H2 adds task Z2 through H2's local CLI. The owner rotates the APNs key and the SMTP password | Stage 1 only. `trust confirm` and v2, v1 and CLI writes on H3 are refused: H2's writer fence is unknown. The isolation note and the rotations change nothing about writes | "Previous Lisa Core on H2 is cut off from your devices (noted <time>) but may still change data on its own: its schedule, connectors and local commands. This Lisa Core accepts no changes until H2 is stopped with restart prevented. APNs and SMTP: credentials rotated." |
| 8 | Fence H2 (stage 1) | The owner reaches H2's console | The owner stops H2's Core and runs `lisa core fence --local --export-tail <dir>` on H2's volume: it takes the lock, writes the schema marker and a terminal journal record, signs a fence record binding H2's final run, journal sequence and head, and exports every operation H2 committed after A's cut, Z2 included. `fence import` on H3 records `fenced-on-host`; the tail is imported as replay candidates | "Previous Lisa Core on H2: fenced on its own host (<time>); its journal ends at entry 4,812 and can't continue. Its changes after backup A were exported for replay." |
| 9 | Confirm E3 (stage 2 → 3) | The ledger copy on H3, device reports of E2's head, H2's tail | Confirmation n+2 for E3 with `supersedes: [E2]`. The Mac and iPhone verify the confirmed sibling supersession, refresh credentials and resubmit what E2 committed (X, Y1, Z). Z2 is replayed from the tail; H2's derived writes are derived again | Mac and iPhone: "Lisa Core was restored again; resending your changes." Owner: "E2 superseded. Replayed from H2: 1 task added on H2." |
| 10 | The iPad returns late, with a key Core doesn't know | The iPad holds E1's head, Y2 pending, roster v9 naming its key D2, and a bearer the restore invalidated. E3's roster still names D1 | Its first request gets `credential_invalidated_by_restore`; its refresh challenge is refused because D2 is unknown. The owner runs `lisa core trust evidence-code`; the iPad uploads its bundle (v9 and the K1→K2 record); Core verifies v9 from A's anchored key history through K1→K2. D2 becomes known; the iPad's challenge succeeds; it refreshes, verifies E3 (confirmed, descending from E1 at A's cut), receives the roster revoking C, and submits Y2; blob b is already present | iPad: "Lisa Core doesn't recognize this device's current key. Ask for a code to send this device's trust records." Owner: "Trust records from iPad: roster v9 verified; iPad's current key accepted." Then "iPad reported; 1 change admitted." |
| 11 | C tries again | C is revoked in the merged roster | Its operations after the revocation are rejected; operations with same-lineage receipts from before it are admitted | "Device C: 1 change rejected (revoked), kept for inspection." |
| 12 | Delivery review (stage 4) | E3's database lacks O's email row and P's assignment. H1's journal is lost, the off-host copy ends before O's acceptance, and H1's attested fence binds no journal entry. H2's fence binds its final entry; its complete journal shows one accepted brief email (slot Q) and nothing for O or P. The iPhone has reported P as scheduled locally | `delivery resume` resumes SMTP and APNs (sender fences known). O stays held: no completeness proof exists for H1. Q stays accepted and is never resent. P becomes device-owned and is never pushed. The owner runs `delivery release O` with acknowledgement; the released attempt's answer is lost in a crash, so O becomes `outcomeUnknown`; the owner finds the reference in the inbox and records `delivery confirm O --received` | "Held: email reminder for <task> — the previous Lisa Core on H1 may already have sent it; no fence binds its last journal entry. Sending again may duplicate it." After the crash: "Released by you; outcome unknown. Reference R-7Q4K: check your inbox for it." After the owner's record: "Delivered (confirmed by you)." |

**Checks at the end:**
- H3 confirmed nothing and admitted no write while H2's writer fence was unknown, although H2 was cut off from every client and the APNs and SMTP credentials had been rotated.
- H2's writes after its isolation reached E3 once: Z2 from the fenced tail; the derived writes derived again.
- No client accepted E2 or E3 before its confirmation, as a descendant or as a sibling.
- X, Y1, Y2, Z and Z2 are applied exactly once in E3; blob b is present once.
- The iPad's new key became known only through its verified bundle, and nothing in the bundle was trusted before verification.
- C is revoked; its post-revocation change is rejected.
- Every device's head is E3. The iPad's refreshed credential works, and its old bearer stays refused.
- P is never pushed, and the iPhone's local alert is the only one. Q is never resent.
- O stays held through regeneration, `resume` and a restart, is sent at most once more, only after the owner's release, and is recorded as delivered only on the owner's own finding.
- No mutation happened before stage 3, and no delivery before stage 4.

### 19.2 A rollback with acknowledgements, work Core committed but a device never learned, a later offline create and edit, overlapping exports, the original device lost, a crash during reconciliation, and an idea created through R1's capture path (N-B)

**Setup.** Core is migrated from R1 and running with a Mac, an iPhone and an iPad. Core's export key was rotated earlier from Kx1 to Kx2. The owner keeps the documented key backup: current and retired export keys and the signing-key history. Shortly before the rollback, the iPad submitted operation 501, "create task T3 'Book the vet'". Core committed it, but the connection dropped before the receipt arrived. The iPad then went offline, still showing 501 as waiting, and changed T3's title (502).

| # | Step | Evidence available | Allowed action | What the owner sees |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Preflight | Device list, the counts each device last reported | `migrate rollback --preflight` creates generation R7 | "Rollback R7: waiting for acknowledgements from Mac, iPhone, iPad." |
| 2 | Acknowledgements | The iPhone syncs and signs `{R7, position 420, all outstanding counts 0}`. The Mac signs `{R7, position 310, quarantined 1}` and exports its quarantined R1 item, so its acknowledgement carries the export digest. The iPad is unreachable | Record acknowledgements; never infer the iPad's | "iPhone: acknowledged through 420 at <time>; nothing outstanding. Mac: acknowledged through 310; 1 quarantined item, exported (<digest>). iPad: no acknowledgement; last reported position 500 with 1 pending, at <time>." |
| 3 | Work after acknowledging | The iPhone, offline, creates task T2 "Call the plumber" (position 421) and edits its title (422). Core never learns of them | Saved locally; prompt to export | iPhone: "Saved on this device. Lisa Core is being rolled back; export this change." |
| 4 | Fence | Acknowledgements; the iPad outstanding | `--fence`: intake fenced; scheduler stopped; in-flight attempts settled; assignment cancels sent; the rollback archive written with the Core-signed roster, Core's final dispositions (501 among them, `accepted`) and the projection map | "Fenced. Outstanding: iPad. Archive written: <path>, digest …" |
| 5 | Reverse migration and verification | The archive and the v5 backup | `--reverse` creates v4, writes the reversed ledger with terminal replies and sanitized errors, and regenerates `runtime-state.json` and `model-requests.json`. `verify-r1 --copy` runs the frozen R1 binary on a copy (the edit history is present; `run-once` sends nothing), then the frozen client, queue and CLI replay reversed request IDs: a capture deleted in R2 answers "already saved", a deleted task's create answers a decodable 409, and nothing runs twice | Rollback report: "iPhone: acknowledged through 420. Mac: through 310, 1 quarantined item exported. iPad: no acknowledgement; last reported position 500. Changes saved on a device after the time shown aren't known to Lisa Core." Conflicts kept; uncertain deliveries became `outcomeUnknown`; the limits after R1's ledger expires |
| 6 | R1 resumes | The verified v4 directory | The owner starts R1 | R1 running; the R2 Core is gone |
| 7 | Two exports | The iPhone holds 421–422, then adds a note to T2 (423) | Protected export to Files (iCloud Drive), sealed to Kx2 and the iPhone's export key and signed by the iPhone; later a second export holding 421–423 | iPhone: "Exported 2 changes …", then "Exported 3 changes …" |
| 8 | iPhone lost | Only the two exported files remain | — | — |
| 9 | Inspect | The rollback archive, both iPhone archives, the key backup; R1's database, read-only | `lisa archive inspect` deduplicates 421 and 422 across the two archives (same IDs and digests). Kx2 from the key backup decrypts them; the iPhone's signature, checked against the rollback archive's Core-signed roster through the key history, authenticates them; the one eligibility rule finds the iPhone active and unrevoked; the rollback-cut lookup finds 421–423 absent (Core never decided them). R1 has no task "Call the plumber" | "3 entries from iPhone (2 in both archives). Decrypted with export key Kx2; signed by iPhone and verified against the rollback roster. Entry 421: create task 'Call the plumber' — not known to Lisa Core before the rollback." |
| 10 | Reconcile 421; both processes killed | The owner approves 421 | The tool records the intent (request ID `lisa-rc-…`, body, "absent", time) and the `sending` mark, then sends `POST /v1/tasks`. R1 commits and is killed before writing its ledger record; the tool is killed before recording completion | Log: "421: sent; no answer." |
| 11 | Restart; the route's proof | An intent with a `sending` mark and no completion; a read-only snapshot of R1's database | No ledger record exists for `owner:lisa-rc-…`, so the create is indeterminate; no time window is used. The same snapshot holds exactly one task with 421's content, T-r1. The owner maps 421 to T-r1, and the mapping is recorded | "Entry 421 (create task 'Call the plumber'): R1 may have created it; R1's record of this request is gone or was never written. One task with exactly this content exists (task T-r1, created <time by R1's clock>). Use this task, send again (may duplicate), or skip." Then: "Mapped to T-r1." |
| 12 | Dependent edits | The mapping | 422 (title) and 423 (note) go through `PATCH /v1/tasks/T-r1` with the revision read from R1, each with its intent recorded first | "Applied in R1: title updated (revision 2); note added (revision 3)." |
| 13 | The iPad is found | The iPad exports 501 and 502, and also an idea created offline (503) with a tag edit (504), sealed to Kx2. An older iPad archive was sealed with Kx1 before the export-key rotation | The rollback cut first: 501 is `accepted` with the same digest, so it becomes `applied_before_rollback` and is never sent; the projection map seeds T3's mapping to its R1 record, and 502 goes to that record with its revision. 503 goes through `POST /v1/captures` with kind `idea`; its answer is lost on a dropped connection, and the safe resend returns the existing capture and idea; the mapping is recorded, and 504's tags go through `PATCH /v1/ideas/{id}`. The older archive opens with retired Kx1 from the backup; its draft waits for the owner | "iPad: 1 change was already in Lisa Core before the rollback ('Book the vet'); 3 entries applied; 1 draft needs your text confirmation." |
| 14 | A revoked device's archive | An archive from device C, revoked before the rollback, carrying an early creation time | The one eligibility rule makes it ineligible in `inspect` and `reconcile` alike; it is listed, and its content can only be re-entered deliberately | "Device C (revoked <time>): 1 entry not eligible. You can re-enter its content yourself." |

**Checks at the end:**
- R1 holds T2 exactly once, with the later title and the note; rerunning `reconcile` repeats nothing.
- R1 holds T3 once — from Core's commit before the rollback — with the iPad's later title; its create was never sent again.
- The entries present in both iPhone archives were reconciled once.
- R1 holds the iPad's idea once, created through R1's capture path, with its tags.
- No R2 scheduler ran after the fence.
- The rollback report named only acknowledged and reported positions; the iPhone's 421–423 appeared nowhere until its exports surfaced them; the iPad was never treated as clean, and the Mac's acknowledgement counted its quarantined item.
- Every executed entry was decrypted with an export key and authenticated by its device's signature against the rollback roster; one eligibility rule answered the same everywhere; C's entry stayed ineligible; drafts waited for the owner.
- The frozen R1 callers decoded every reply from the reversed ledger, and nothing ran twice.
- `lisa archive log` shows each intent, the indeterminate create, the owner's mapping, the safe resend and the entry applied before the rollback.
- Recovery used only the rollback archive, the device archives and the documented key backup, in a fresh directory.

## 22. Owner decisions, open decisions and unavailable evidence, by gate

**The owner's decisions of 2026-11-22, now settled, and their evidence triggers**

| Decision | Settled as | Evidence that triggers the next step | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| P-08 swift-certificates | Conditional. Only if the physical N-1 proof selects mutual TLS does Claude bring the exact commit, the deciding proof, toolchain and lockfile compatibility, and the package and lockfile changes; the owner approves the exact pin before addition; X1's proof-only provisioning stays separate. Otherwise inapplicable | N-1's recorded result at X1 | Apple clients (nearby) |
| Apple Developer account and APNs | The owner alone signs, provisions and runs the apps and live Apple checks; Claude is denied the account; the owner-run harness carries signing, registration, entitlements, profiles, APNs keys and token setup in its preflight; credentials stay on the owner's systems; device availability is not verification | X6 and LS-3, run by the owner | Apple clients (R2-A10), required integrations |
| Required integrations and account access | Mail and calendar on personal Outlook.com, Gmail with Google Calendar, iCloud and Proton; OpenAI, Anthropic and a self-hosted Ollama on Core and as the Apple fallback; Twilio Voice. No owner account, credential, session or connected-account tool for Claude; the owner runs every credentialed check; calendars only with authenticated, private, read-only access | LS-1, LS-2, LS-3 and X5, run by the owner | Required integrations |
| E-1 | A release requirement. A failing platform keeps its gates open; a compliant redesign within the destination policy is planned and proved; deferral to Core is only a runtime fallback; only a redesign needing a new permission, dependency or requirement change comes back | X1, then X5 on each platform | Apple clients, R2 release |
| N-1 | A release requirement: working nearby sync on Mac–iPhone, Mac–iPad and iPhone–iPad, with shared Wi-Fi, Internet disconnected and no access point; if both designs fail, the gates stay open and a compliant redesign repeats the proofs; P-08 stays a separate approval | X1, then X4 | Apple clients, R2 release |

**Newly evidenced blockers that need the owner's decision**

| Item | Primary-source evidence (read 2026-11-22, §1.5) | Unmet capability | Gate affected | The owner's options | Until decided |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Proton Calendar** | "Proton Calendar doesn't support CalDAV, so you can't set up a direct two-way sync with an external calendar. The only option right now is to subscribe to a calendar via a shared link" (proton.me support, "subscribe-to-external-calendar"); "Anyone with the shared calendar link can view the calendar", and a full-view link carries the decryption key ("share-calendar-via-link"); Bridge serves mail only; Proton's 2026 roadmaps announce no calendar API, CalDAV or Bridge calendar | Authenticated, private, read-only access by a third-party client. Proton's only external route is a link anyone holding it can open, which the owner's requirement excludes | Required integrations (the Proton Calendar row), and therefore R2 release | Keep the requirement, leaving the row and the release gate open until Proton offers such an interface; or change it — for example, remove Proton Calendar from the required matrix, or name another mechanism to accept | The row stays open: `NOT_RUN_ENVIRONMENT`, provider capability missing (owner and Claude) |
| **P-09: Proton Mail Bridge** | Bridge is Proton's only documented IMAP and SMTP route; it serves only "applications running on the same device"; it needs a paid plan, a password store and an interactive login; its Linux packages are 64-bit x86 only | A Proton Mail route that needs no new component | Required integrations (the Proton Mail rows) | Approve a Bridge sidecar sharing Core's network namespace (Claude would bring the exact version, its official package and signature, and the sidecar definition for review); or run Bridge yourself where Core can reach it, which is host administration outside Claude's scope; or change the requirement | Implementation `BLOCKED_PERMISSION` (Claude); the live cases wait |

**Conditional, decided by evidence still to come** (no decision is needed now):

| Item | If the evidence fails | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| E-13: Graph reading a calendar one personal Microsoft account shares with another | The row stays open. The only other authenticated route is authorizing the owner's own account with `Calendars.Read`, which reaches the owner's personal account; that comes to the owner as a decision then | Required integrations (Outlook.com calendar) |
| E-14: iCloud CalDAV with an app-specific password, and shared calendars visible there | The row stays open with the evidence; Apple documents no other route | Required integrations (iCloud Calendar) |

**Revision 6 needs no other decision.** Its rules stay within the required behavior, the owner's trust policy and the existing permissions: leaf pinning is kept, and CA-only renewal isn't proposed; the dedicated-account design keeps R2-SCOPE-02 and `CONSTRICTION.md`'s rule against turning subscribed sources into account access; the owner harness, the controlled local Ollama server and every provider adapter use only approved dependencies, and the harness changes no lockfile.

**Evidence unavailable now, with its executor and cause**

| Evidence | Status — executor — cause | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| arm64 image build and run | `NOT_RUN_ENVIRONMENT` — Claude — missing hardware or platform (no owner commitment) | Container deployment (passes for amd64 only) |
| SELinux confinement on this host's engine | `NOT_RUN_ENVIRONMENT` — Claude — environment configuration (no SELinux labelling) | Container deployment (target host needed) |
| The positive rootless publish case | Needs a private non-loopback address already assigned to this host, checked read-only at M4; if none exists, `NOT_RUN_ENVIRONMENT` — Claude — environment configuration, while the refusals still run | Container deployment (rootless part) |
| Target-host VPN and off-VPN checks, SELinux on the target | `NOT_RUN_ENVIRONMENT` — owner — target-host session pending | Container deployment |
| Apple builds and device checks (X1–X7), N-1 and E-1 included | `NOT_RUN_ENVIRONMENT` — owner — owner sessions pending | Apple clients |
| Live APNs (X6) | `NOT_RUN_ENVIRONMENT` — owner — session pending; or `NOT_RUN_CREDENTIALS` — owner — profiles or the APNs key not yet provisioned | Apple clients, required integrations |
| Live mail and calendars per provider (LS-1, LS-3) | `NOT_RUN_ENVIRONMENT` — owner — sessions pending; or `NOT_RUN_CREDENTIALS` — owner — the dedicated account, app registration, OAuth client or app-specific password not yet provisioned | Required integrations |
| Graph shared calendars (E-13); iCloud CalDAV (E-14) | `NOT_RUN_ENVIRONMENT` — owner — LS-1 pending; the providers don't document them | Required integrations |
| Live models on Core and devices (LS-2, X5) | `NOT_RUN_ENVIRONMENT` — owner — sessions pending | Required integrations |
| The live Twilio call (LS-3) | `NOT_RUN_ENVIRONMENT` — owner — session pending | Required integrations |

| Proton Calendar | `NOT_RUN_ENVIRONMENT` — owner and Claude — provider capability missing (decision above) | Required integrations, R2 release |

Claude's lack of access to the owner's accounts is not an item in this table: it is a standing boundary, and no case that needs an owner account is assigned to Claude.

### Revision 6 (2026-11-23, proposed)

- **Reviewed:** revision 5 at `8534ea6`; revision 4 at `b2f67db`; R1 at `491c90a`. The checkout had not moved (HEAD `8534ea6`, clean worktree, four local commits ahead of `origin/feature/distributive`). The optional "R2 revision 5 approval review.md" isn't on this account's filesystem; the owner's self-contained brief was used. Temporary work from earlier sessions is preserved untouched.
- **Evidence:** R1 facts added to §1.6 from the preference, capture, retention, ledger, error and adapter code; `Sources/`, `Apps/`, `Tests/` and the package files unchanged since `491c90a`; `Package.resolved` re-hashed and unchanged. Provider documentation for Microsoft, Google, Apple iCloud, Proton, OpenAI, Anthropic, Ollama and Twilio read on 2026-11-22 through delegated, read-only research of public pages (§1.5). Nothing was built or run, and no account was used.
- **The five blockers** (front matter table):
  1. writer fences that stop every write path and every restart (client isolation only a note); `fence --local` binding final positions and exporting the unsynced tail; owner-mediated trust bundles for devices whose keys Core doesn't know, quarantined and verified from an independent anchor (§8.5, §8.1.5, §8.4.4);
  2. delivery completeness only through a fence that binds the old writer's final run, sequence and chain hash; open claims matched by `claimID`; what the owner can establish about a released attempt (§8.10.6);
  3. immediate-predecessor chains; held administrative children with the suffix pending behind a CLI-only decision and a consumable 503; outcome-only migrated parents; the R1 fingerprint kept at Core; redacted receipts for restricted siblings (§8.12.2, §8.2.1, §8.14.4);
  4. capture verdicts over every effect-bearing input; task-create proof only from R1's own ledger; the rollback cut consulted first; per-kind preference rules; decodable terminal replies in the reversed ledger (§8.14.3, §8.14.7, §8.14.8, §8.12.2);
  5. deferred pages, invisible staging and a constant-size publication that re-checks epoch, coverage generation and cursors; membership records deciding absence across partitions (§8.4.2, §8.4.3, §8.6.3).
- **Related corrections** (§21): unanchored backups for inspection only, decisions versus replay bytes and per-holder coverage; reviewed-set artifacts in exports and resolutions waiting for them; error templates without caller text; A records only on Apple devices; exact-target OS operations, confirmation-ordered generations and the high-water mark; rollback reports of acknowledged and reported positions only, one eligibility rule, decryption versus authentication.
- **Owner decisions recorded** (§15, §16, §22): P-08 conditional on N-1, with the exact-pin review; the Apple Developer account and APNs used only by the owner; the required integrations, with no owner account for Claude; E-1 and N-1 as release requirements; the O-6 extension for the owner-verification harness.
- **New designs:** required integrations per provider with dedicated Lisa accounts (§8.19); the owner-verification harness, its entry points, modes, results, redaction and delivery schedule (§10.8); the Apple and provider acceptance matrices with result levels, executors and causes (§10.9, §10.1); owner live sessions LS-1–LS-3 and experiments E-13–E-15 (§10.5, §10.6).
- **New modules:** `LisaProviderAuth`, `LisaMicrosoftGraph`, `LisaGoogleAPIs`, `LisaCalDAV`, `LisaAnthropic` and `LisaOllama` (M7); the owner-harness targets under `Harness/` and `Apps/OwnerVerification/` (M1 on).
- **Newly evidenced blockers for the owner** (§22): Proton Calendar has no compliant route; Proton Mail needs the Bridge sidecar (P-09).
- **Corrections to revision 5:** `owner-attested-isolated` as a writer fence (isolation doesn't stop local writes); `clean-shutdown` as proof of a journal's end; release from a complete suffix while a claim open at the cut had no outcome; a dependency on every earlier same-key child; later children running past a held administrative child; `r1Fingerprint` in replicated envelopes; capture verdicts without the title; a five-minute wall-clock window as proof that a create failed; `{}` as a reversed terminal reply; recorded errors assumed free of caller input; native AAAA answers trusted after negative discovery; an old cancel able to remove a newer firing's request; late-entry positions in the rollback record.
